Riemann hypothesis

Prove the Riemann hypothesis: every zero \(\rho\) of the meromorphic Riemann zeta function with \(0<\Re\rho<1\) has \(\Re\rho=1/2\).

Origin: starting research record.

Starting point

User-created RH thread, 11 October 2026, starting from the OpenAI zero-free import and Anthropic density proof import. Those notebooks own the complete records, attribution, source snapshots and verification evidence; this notebook owns the RH goal. Setup is not a completed research cycle.

Where we stand

Goal. RH remains open. No zero is excluded; the imports are unaudited.

Imported position. thm:openai-seven-eighths-half-plane reports zero-freeness for \(\Re s>7/8\); thm:anthropic-critical-line-density reports simple-critical density \(0.67250\ldots\) and distinct density \(0.83625\ldots\). Both are unverified; in the half-plane's intermediate preprint no error was found in what was read (status and unread parts). lem:hermitian-rank-trace has a reviewed proof.

Gram model. On a complete grid, an off-line pair at distance \(\delta\) contributes eigenvalues \(\kappa+1\), \(-(\kappa-1)\), \(\kappa\approx e^{\delta\Lambda}\) at bandwidth \(\Lambda\), and the trace of two on-line zeros (pair atom spectrum). \(\|G\|_{\mathrm{HS}}^2\) is the mean square of \(\sum_\rho x^{\rho-1/2}\) over \(|\log x|\le\Lambda\) (many-pair bound). Real configurations with a local count satisfy a second-moment bound at every bandwidth; conversely such a bound over a range of bandwidths of length \(4M^2/(gm_*^2)\) bounds the largest \(\delta\) at distinct heights, though at one bandwidth close pairs can cancel (resonance-free bandwidth); at \(2n\) lattice steps a cluster's sum is bounded below with a loss depending on the gaps (power sums, gap-free). The need for bandwidth beyond \(\log T\) is a property of the second moment: a solitary pair moves \(\lambda_{\max}\) to at least \(\kappa+1\) and \(\lambda_{\min}\) to at most \(-(\kappa-1)+\eta\), \(\eta\) bounded by local counts of real points, so an eigenvalue bound \(T^{\varepsilon}\) at bandwidth \(\log T\) would give \(\delta\le\varepsilon+o(1)\) (extreme eigenvalue detection); the import controls two moments only. On the prime side the form is \(\int H_c^2\nu_X\); an elementary bound gives only the trivial \(\sqrt X\), and whether the eigenvalue premise is the goal in local form is not established (prime-side form). The density source has \(X=(T/2\pi)^\lambda\), \(\lambda\le1\); its off-diagonal prime bound \(O(L^2X)\) is where \(X\le T\) is used, and at that strength the second moment at bandwidth \(\theta\log T\) is about \(T^\theta\), giving \(\delta\le1/2\), nothing at any bandwidth; a saving \(T^{-\varepsilon}\) would give \(\delta\le1/2-\varepsilon/(2\theta)\) (bandwidth reading).

Continuation mechanism. The seven-eighths source's criterion: a low bound for a probe and a saving, relative to the supremum \(\beta_*\) of zeros, for its distance from a Mellin integral of the reciprocal \(L\)-function (criterion reading). A zero is a pole there, so close zeros cannot cancel; but the criterion is also a reformulation, the content being a probe built without knowing the zeros. In the source's family of scales the boundary is \(11/12-\ell/4\), \(\ell\) the prime-slot length (\(1/6\) at \(7/8\)), with a low estimate stated for \(3\ell/2+4b/3\le1/2\) and, under the reading's extrapolations, a high-side floor \(13/15+b/30\) for the boundary; the binding inequality is the endpoint certificate, margin about \(2\cdot10^{-4}\) (constraint reading, corrected by the next link). The second stage needs the earlier bound as a threshold and is not improved by a better one; under listed extrapolations its scales are within \(5\cdot10^{-5}\) of the family's optimum, and perfect row counts would gain at most \(1/120\) (family optimum). A lower boundary needs a different probe.

Route decisions. Target: exclusion of one off-line zero. Three route reviews (first, second, third) found every mechanism examined to be a reformulation lacking a power saving that does not presuppose the zeros' location; the continuation mechanism has no slack in its family; a certificate with positivity on the whole strip, or relaxed in a window with one count, can exclude a point only where the weighted count of zeros near its height is below \(4/\cos(\pi\delta)\) (strip positivity obstruction, relaxed). A property \(\xi\) shares with a backward heat-flowed function cannot be the only one a proof uses (heat flow, its series). The Hermite–Biehler and Li criteria are the zero-free statement (sign, structure, Li). The conditional exclusion for \(\zeta\) from prime sums at one height rediscovers Turán's theorem under Lindelöf; the import's extraction stops at \(1-1/(2k)\) (fourth review).

Open statements

  1. Off-line exclusion — open. For every \(\rho\in\mathbb C\) with \(\zeta(\rho)=0\) and \(0<\Re\rho<1\), prove \(\Re\rho=1/2\). Every finite height and arbitrarily sparse exceptions must be covered; no simplicity conclusion is required. This is the necessary endpoint, still unproved by the imported density and fixed half-plane statements.

The remaining route

  1. Highest risk: individual off-line exclusion. Identify an exact inequality or contradiction that rules out a fixed hypothetical off-line zero, including in \(1/2<\Re\rho\le7/8\). Test any density, inertia or detector extension against this requirement before refining constants. In the Gram model the two missing parts are a second-moment bound beyond bandwidth \(\log T\) and control of cancellation among close off-line pairs; the first is a mean-square bound for the zero sum and follows from the goal in the model; the second is settled for separated pairs and open for clusters.
  2. Validate selected inputs. Before mathematical reliance, audit the indispensable density moment/localization estimates or the selected OpenAI zero-detector premise and analytic identification. The density record's grid-count discrepancy and cosine-window sandwich warning remain live. Retaining Lean sources and upstream audit reports does not constitute local replay.
  3. Choose by the missing implication. Compare a repair or strengthening of the imported methods with a direct finite-height positivity/detector approach. Demand coverage of every zero; do not turn asymptotic density into exclusion without a proved bridge.

Proposed next step

Route item: rh-exclusion. Queue item: the queue's head after the real points premise, registry-collision-divided-differences, whose queued text asks to "expand the form in divided differences of the kernel at the cluster and read off the exact order of the cancellation loss". Next: read the two registered statements it names (lem:cube-pair-collision-valuation and prop:cube-cluster-order-loss) in full from their records, and decide whether the question is still open after the entries on clusters: the single-bandwidth loss of order \(b^{-2k}\) on the subset-sum family, the short resonance-free range, and the threshold failure interval. If those answer it as far as the route can use, say so and close it with its stakes; if the divided-difference expansion gives the exact order of the single-bandwidth loss for a general cluster of \(n\) pairs, between the exponents \(2\log_2n\) and \(2(n-1)\) left undetermined, state and prove that. This is the sixth research entry since the fifth review; the sixth route review follows it, with a Builds paragraph and a judgement on the order in which the completed sum decay was reported.

Working mathematical context

Objects. Use the analytic meromorphic \(\zeta(s)\); the pole at \(s=1\) and trivial zeros are outside the goal. Nontrivial zeros are points in \(0<\Re s<1\), with analytic multiplicity \(m_\rho\ge1\). Any reduction by reflection/conjugation must retain its precise hypotheses and cover all heights.

Imported conventions. \(N(T_1,T_2)=\sum_{T_1<\Im\rho\le T_2}m_\rho\). The simple-critical numerator counts points of multiplicity one with real part \(1/2\); distinct counts include off-line points separately. The density proof pairs \(\rho\) with \(1-\bar\rho\), uses \(z_\rho=(\rho-1/2)/i\), and compresses a real/complex Hermitian Weil form. Its reviewed matrix inequality is \(\operatorname{rank}P\ge2\operatorname{tr}P+4\operatorname{tr}Q-4b-\|P+Q\|_{\mathrm{HS}}^2\) for \(P\succeq0\), \(n_+(Q)\le b\). Its number-theory deduction still depends on the exact trace and second-moment estimates in the linked full record.

Gram model. For a real even \(\varphi\in L^2\) supported in \([-\Lambda/2,\Lambda/2]\), grid step \(2\pi/\Lambda\) over all of \(\mathbb Z\), \(v_z=(\widehat\varphi(z-\alpha_k))_k\) and \(N_\varphi=\Lambda\|\varphi\|_2^2\): \(v_z^{\mathsf T}v_w=\Lambda\widehat{\varphi^2}(z-w)\), \(v_z^*v_w=\Lambda\widehat{\varphi^2}(w-\bar z)\), \(\kappa(\delta)=\int\varphi^2\cosh(2\delta u)/\int\varphi^2\). For the flat window the normalized bilinear Gram is \(\operatorname{sinc}((z-w)\Lambda/2)\). These are statements about finite conjugation-closed point configurations; transfer to the import's cut grid needs the tail hypothesis of solitary amplification, part 5, which is unverified for its window.

Prime sums. Read in Turán's 1984 book, Theorem 33.2: with \(Z(\tau,N_1,N_2)=\sum_{N_1\le n\le N_2}\Lambda(n)e^{-i\tau\log n}\), a bound \(|Z(\tau_0,N_1,N_2)|<cN\log^{100}N/\tau_0^{\,b}\) on all \(\tau_0^{a}\le N\le N_1<N_2\le2N\) (\(a>0\), \(0<b\le1\)) gives \(\zeta\ne0\) on \(\sigma\ge1-b^3/a^2\), \(t=\tau_0\). The implication is lossy (third review), and as proved for its class of Dirichlet series never gives width above \(1/1600\): the proof takes \(s_0=1+\mu^*+i\tau_0\) with \(\mu^*\) tiny, compares a derivative of order about \(a\log\tau_0\) of \(f'/f\) there with the zeros within \(8\mu^*\), and pays the second main theorem for all of them, their number known only from Jensen's formula (where it loses). With the parameters free (long-sum converse): for an abstract \(f\) with \(O(\log\tau_0)\) zeros and growth \(O(\log\tau_0)\) in a fixed disk, a saving \(\tau_0^{-\vartheta a}\) on all lengths above \(\tau_0^{a}\), \(a\) large, excludes zeros at that height with \(1-\beta<\vartheta\). For \(\zeta\) that premise is false once \(\vartheta a>1\): the pole gives the sums a term of size \(y/\tau_0\). At the exponents the pole allows (sparse off-line exclusion): for \(0<x<1/2\) there are \(\mu\), \(R_1=\mu+\tfrac14+\tfrac x2\), \(\kappa>0\) such that for large \(\tau_0\), if \(|\sum_{y<n\le y'}\Lambda(n)n^{-i\tau_0}|\le Cy\tau_0^{-1}\log^{A}y\) for \(\tau_0^{2}\le y<y'\le2y\) and at most \(\kappa\log\tau_0\) zeros lie in \(|s-(1+\mu+i\tau_0)|\le R_1\) (a disk in \(\Re s\ge\tfrac34-\tfrac x2\)), then \(\zeta(1-x+i\tau_0)\ne0\). The premise is unproved; \(\kappa\) is small. With the count only on the shell of zeros within \(e^{\varepsilon}\) of the least distance (shell version): at \(x=1/4\), centre \(4.125+i\tau_0\), more than \(0.02\log\tau_0\) zeros within two percent of the least distance, valid only beyond about \(\tau_0=e^{460}\). Theorem 8.1 there: for \(1=|z_1|\ge\dots\ge|z_n|\), \(m\ge0\), \(\max_{m<\nu\le m+n}|\sum b_jz_j^\nu|\ge2(n/(8e(m+n)))^n\min_j|b_1+\dots+b_j|\).

The import's device. Read in the outline of its eleven-twelfths preprint, unaudited (family extraction): the premise is a power saving for \(A_1(D)=\sum\mu(\mathfrak n)\nu(\mathfrak n)W(N\mathfrak n/D)\) at every scale and cutoff, with no uniformity in the height; the sum is embedded in \(A_u(D)\) with the sextic symbol \(\chi_{\mathfrak n}(u)\), recurs at \(u=p^6\), and a mean square \(\sum_{N(u)\le H}|A_u|^2\ll D^{1+\epsilon}H\), \(H=D^{1+\vartheta}\), proved through Poisson summation, a Gauss-sum identity absorbing \(\mu\), and the cubic theta function, gives \(A_1\ll D^{11/12+}\). With a \(2m\)-th moment over \(D^{\beta}\) rows the extraction gives \(\tfrac12+\beta(1-\tfrac1k)/(2m)\): at best \(1-1/(2k)\) for \(\beta\ge m\), and for \(\beta<m\) the moment hypothesis alone already gives every member \(\tfrac12+\beta/(2m)\) (moment extraction). The preprint reduces its mean square to a dual one for cubic Gauss coefficients \(\overline{\alpha(n)}\gamma_2(n)\xi(n)\) twisted by \(\chi_n(k)\) over unrestricted rows \(k\), claimed of size \(D^{\epsilon}XF\) for \(\mathcal H\le XFD^{-\kappa}\); the hinge is whether a polar term survives in the completed sums of theta coefficients (dual mean square). Rule for this route: every mean square over unrestricted rows is first evaluated on the sixth-power, cube and square rows, where the symbol is trivial, quadratic or cubic.

Source accounting. The strongest directly relevant imported fixed zero-free boundary is \(7/8\), with strict inequality and no boundary exclusion. It does not imply the \(1/2\) boundary. The separate real-character theorem concerns \((1-\beta)\log q\ge c\), and the cyclotomic statement has width \(10^{-6}\); neither is used as an RH premise. All inherited claim IDs retain their original registry status and source links. Complete licensed density proof sources and the OpenAI source snapshot remain available in this checkout.

Gaps identified by formalization

No new formalization is assigned or executed. Imported number-theory conclusions remain statement-level inspections here. The density import includes its complete licensed Lean project, with upstream audits but no local build, comparator replay or axiom audit. The OpenAI import contains selected entry/scope files rather than the full transitive proof tree; its custom Hecke definition/source correspondence remains unresolved. The analytic density prose discrepancies are recorded audit questions, not kernel-check results. A Lean function totalized at a pole must not silently replace the analytic meromorphic convention.

Research record

11 October 2026 — One off-line zero in the density proof's Gram form: its exact spectrum, a bound for a solitary zero, and a two-pair cancellation showing the bound is not additive

Working proofs, and a refutation of an additive lower bound over pairs, in an abstract complete-grid model; nothing is proved about actual zeta zeros. First bridge test. Produced by Claude Code (claude-opus-5-5, low effort).

Question. The open statement is off-line exclusion; the missing implication is from the information the imported density proof controls (trace and Hilbert–Schmidt norm of its Gram form, positive-index counts) to the absence of one fixed zero with \(\Re\rho>1/2\). This cycle computes exactly what such a zero contributes to that form and asks which estimate would contradict it. The test ends with a conditional bridge with explicit premises or with a precise statement of the missing information.

New mathematics. Three elementary statements about the Gram form, proved below for every window and bandwidth. (1) A conjugate pair off the real axis contributes two nonzero eigenvalues, \(\kappa+1\) and \(-(\kappa-1)\), where \(\kappa\) grows like \(e^{\delta\Lambda}\) in the bandwidth \(\Lambda\) and the distance \(\delta\) from the line; its trace is the same as for two on-line zeros. (2) If the pair is the only one off the axis, the largest eigenvalue and the Hilbert–Schmidt norm of the whole form are at least \(\kappa+1\). (3) For two pairs the bound is not additive: at height difference \(\pi/\Lambda\) their leading terms cancel up to a factor of order \((\delta\Lambda)^{-2}\). No statement about the zeros of \(\zeta\) follows without further premises.

Goal progress. Unchanged. The outcome of the test is the precise missing information, in two parts. First, at the bandwidth \(\Lambda=\log(T/2\pi)\) of the imported second-moment premise, the solitary bound gives \(\delta\le1/2+O(\log\log T/\log T)\), which every zero satisfies already: this bound, applied to a solitary pair on the complete grid, gives no constraint at \(\theta=1\); the premise might constrain zeros by other routes. By this route a constraint needs a second-moment bound at bandwidth \(\theta\log T\) with \(\theta>1\), which the import does not contain. Second, the passage from a solitary pair to several pairs is unproved. The only loss proved here is polylogarithmic (two pairs, a factor of order \((\delta\Lambda)^{-2}\)), which would not change the exponent; whether many close pairs can lose a power of \(T\) is untested. Neither part is supplied here.

General statement. prop:solitary-amplification, for every bandwidth \(\Lambda>0\), every real even window \(\varphi\in L^2\) supported in \([-\Lambda/2,\Lambda/2]\) and every finite conjugation-closed multiset of points with exactly one pair off the real axis.

Setting

Fix \(\Lambda>0\), a real number \(\alpha_0\), the grid \(\alpha_k=\alpha_0+2\pi k/\Lambda\) for \(k\in\mathbb Z\), and a real, even, nonzero \(\varphi\in L^2(\mathbb R)\) supported in \(I=[-\Lambda/2,\Lambda/2]\). Use \(\widehat f(z)=\int f(u)e^{-iuz}\,du\) for \(z\in\mathbb C\), and put \(N_\varphi=\Lambda\|\varphi\|_2^2\). For \(z\in\mathbb C\) let \(v_z=(\widehat\varphi(z-\alpha_k))_{k\in\mathbb Z}\). A configuration is a finite set \(Z\subset\mathbb C\) closed under complex conjugation with multiplicities \(m_z=m_{\bar z}\ge1\); its Gram form is \[G_Z={1\over N_\varphi}\sum_{z\in Z}m_zv_zv_z^{\mathsf T}.\] This is the complete-grid version of the form \(G\) of the density import, where \(\Lambda=L=\log(T/2\pi)\), \(z_\rho=\gamma-i(\beta-1/2)\), the partner \(1-\bar\rho\) has the conjugate \(z\), \(N_\varphi=aL^2\), and the grid is cut to \(0\le k<d\). Zeros on the line are real points; a zero with \(\beta=1/2+\delta\) is the point \(\gamma-i\delta\). The import's window \(\chi(L/2+u)\chi(L/2-u)\sqrt{\psi(u/L)}\) with \(\psi\) even is real and even, as assumed here.

Pair atom spectrum

Lemma (pair atom spectrum). lem:pair-atom-spectrum. In the setting above every \(v_z\) lies in \(\ell^2(\mathbb Z)\), \(\overline{v_z}=v_{\bar z}\), and for all \(z,w\in\mathbb C\) \[v_z^{\mathsf T}v_w=\Lambda\,\widehat{\varphi^2}(z-w),\qquad v_z^{*}v_w=\Lambda\,\widehat{\varphi^2}(w-\bar z).\] Consequently, for \(z=\gamma-i\delta\) with \(\delta\ne0\), with \[\kappa(\delta)={\int\varphi(u)^2\cosh(2\delta u)\,du\over\int\varphi(u)^2\,du}>1,\] one has \(v_z^{\mathsf T}v_z=N_\varphi\), \(v_z^*v_z=\kappa N_\varphi\), and there are orthonormal real vectors \(e_x,e_y\in\ell^2(\mathbb Z)\) with \[{1\over N_\varphi}\bigl(v_zv_z^{\mathsf T}+v_{\bar z}v_{\bar z}^{\mathsf T}\bigr)=(\kappa+1)\,e_xe_x^{\mathsf T}-(\kappa-1)\,e_ye_y^{\mathsf T}.\] For real \(z\) the vector \(v_z\) is real and \(v_zv_z^{\mathsf T}/N_\varphi\) is a rank-one orthogonal projection.

Proof. Put \(F_z(u)=\varphi(u)e^{-iuz}\in L^2(I)\) and \(\varepsilon_k(u)=\Lambda^{-1/2}e^{-i\alpha_ku}\). The \(\varepsilon_k\) are the standard Fourier basis of \(L^2(I)\) multiplied by the unimodular function \(e^{-i\alpha_0u}\), hence an orthonormal basis for \(\langle f,g\rangle=\int_If\bar g\). Since \(v_z(k)=\int F_z(u)e^{i\alpha_ku}\,du=\Lambda^{1/2}\langle F_z,\varepsilon_k\rangle\), Parseval gives \(v_z\in\ell^2\) and \[v_z^*v_w=\Lambda\sum_k\overline{\langle F_z,\varepsilon_k\rangle}\langle F_w,\varepsilon_k\rangle=\Lambda\langle F_w,F_z\rangle=\Lambda\int\varphi^2e^{-iuw}e^{iu\bar z}\,du=\Lambda\widehat{\varphi^2}(w-\bar z).\] Next, \(\overline{v_{\bar z}(k)}=\int\varphi(u)e^{iuz}e^{-i\alpha_ku}\,du\), and the substitution \(u\mapsto-u\) with \(\varphi\) even turns this into \(v_z(k)\). So \(\overline{v_z}=v_{\bar z}\) and \(v_z^{\mathsf T}v_w=v_{\bar z}^*v_w=\Lambda\widehat{\varphi^2}(w-z)=\Lambda\widehat{\varphi^2}(z-w)\), the last step because \(\varphi^2\) is even. Now let \(z=\gamma-i\delta\). Then \(v_z^{\mathsf T}v_z=\Lambda\widehat{\varphi^2}(0)=N_\varphi\), and \(z-\bar z=-2i\delta\) gives \(v_z^*v_z=\Lambda\int\varphi^2e^{-2\delta u}\,du=\Lambda\int\varphi^2\cosh(2\delta u)\,du=\kappa N_\varphi\), by evenness again. Since \(\cosh(2\delta u)>1\) for \(u\ne0\) and \(\varphi\ne0\), \(\kappa>1\). Write \(v_z=x+iy\) with real \(x,y\). Then \(v_z^{\mathsf T}v_z=|x|^2-|y|^2+2i\,x\cdot y\) and \(v_z^*v_z=|x|^2+|y|^2\), so \(x\cdot y=0\), \(|x|^2=(\kappa+1)N_\varphi/2\) and \(|y|^2=(\kappa-1)N_\varphi/2>0\). As \(v_{\bar z}=x-iy\), \(v_zv_z^{\mathsf T}+v_{\bar z}v_{\bar z}^{\mathsf T}=2(xx^{\mathsf T}-yy^{\mathsf T})\); take \(e_x=x/|x|\), \(e_y=y/|y|\). For real \(z\), \(v_z=\overline{v_z}\) is real with \(|v_z|^2=N_\varphi\). \(\square\)

The bilinear identity is the import's Poisson identity; what is added is its Hermitian companion at complex arguments and the resulting spectrum. Every hypothesis on \(\varphi\) is used: support in \(I\) for the basis expansion; realness in the Parseval step (\(\varphi^2\) rather than \(|\varphi|^2\)) and, with evenness, for \(\overline{v_z}=v_{\bar z}\); evenness also for the bilinear identity, which without it reads \(\Lambda\int\varphi(u)\varphi(-u)e^{-iu(z-w)}\,du\); and \(\varphi\ne0\) for \(\kappa>1\).

Solitary amplification

Proposition (solitary amplification). prop:solitary-amplification. Let \(Z\) be a configuration with \(p\) conjugate pairs \(\{z_j,\bar z_j\}\) off the real axis, \(z_j=\gamma_j-i\delta_j\), \(\delta_j>0\), with \(\kappa_j=\kappa(\delta_j)\) and vectors \(e_{x_j},e_{y_j}\) from the lemma.

  1. Trace. \(\operatorname{tr}G_Z=\sum_{z\in Z}m_z\), whatever the positions of the points.
  2. Negative index. \(G_Z\) has at most \(p\) negative eigenvalues.
  3. One pair against the others. For each \(j_0\), \[\lambda_{\max}(G_Z)\ge e_{x_{j_0}}^{\mathsf T}G_Ze_{x_{j_0}}\ge m_{j_0}(\kappa_{j_0}+1)-\sum_{j\ne j_0}m_j(\kappa_j-1)\,(e_{x_{j_0}}\cdot e_{y_j})^2.\]
  4. Solitary pair. If \(p=1\), then \(\|G_Z\|_{\mathrm{HS}}\ge\lambda_{\max}(G_Z)\ge m_1(\kappa_1+1)\).
  5. Truncated grid. Let \(\Pi\) be the coordinate projection onto any set of grid indices, \(p=1\), \(\kappa_1\ge2\), and suppose \(\|(1-\Pi)v_{z_1}\|^2\le\epsilon^2\|v_{z_1}\|^2\) with \(\epsilon\le1/4\). Then \(\|\Pi G_Z\Pi\|_{\mathrm{HS}}\ge\lambda_{\max}(\Pi G_Z\Pi)\ge\tfrac45m_1(\kappa_1+1)\).
  6. Growth. If \(\varphi^2\ge c>0\) almost everywhere on \(s\le|u|\le s'\) with \(0\le s<s'\), then \(\kappa(\delta)\ge c\,(s'-s)\,e^{2|\delta|s}/\|\varphi\|_2^2\).

Proof. (1) Each \(v_zv_z^{\mathsf T}\) has rank one and trace \(v_z^{\mathsf T}v_z=N_\varphi\) by the lemma. (2) and (3) Group the sum by real points and pairs. The lemma gives \[G_Z=\sum_{z\in Z\cap\mathbb R}{m_z\over N_\varphi}v_zv_z^{\mathsf T}+\sum_{j=1}^pm_j\bigl[(\kappa_j+1)e_{x_j}e_{x_j}^{\mathsf T}-(\kappa_j-1)e_{y_j}e_{y_j}^{\mathsf T}\bigr],\] a real symmetric finite-rank operator. It is a positive semidefinite operator minus the positive semidefinite operator \(\sum_jm_j(\kappa_j-1)e_{y_j}e_{y_j}^{\mathsf T}\) of rank at most \(p\), so it is nonnegative on a subspace of codimension at most \(p\), which gives (2). Evaluating the quadratic form at the unit vector \(e_{x_{j_0}}\), dropping the nonnegative terms other than the \(j_0\) one and using \(e_{x_{j_0}}\cdot e_{y_{j_0}}=0\) gives (3). (4) is (3) with an empty sum, and \(\|G\|_{\mathrm{HS}}^2\) is the sum of the squared eigenvalues. (5) Write \(e_x,e_y,\kappa,m\) for the pair. Since \(|x|^2\ge|v|^2/2\) and, as \(\kappa\ge2\), \(|y|^2=(\kappa-1)N_\varphi/2\ge\kappa N_\varphi/4=|v|^2/4\), and \(|(1-\Pi)x|,|(1-\Pi)y|\le|(1-\Pi)v|\) coordinatewise, we get \(|(1-\Pi)e_x|^2\le2\epsilon^2\) and \(|(1-\Pi)e_y|^2\le4\epsilon^2\). Let \(u=\Pi e_x/|\Pi e_x|\), where \(|\Pi e_x|^2\ge1-2\epsilon^2\). The compressed on-line terms stay nonnegative, \(e_x\cdot\Pi e_x=|\Pi e_x|^2\), and \(e_y\cdot\Pi e_x=-((1-\Pi)e_y)\cdot((1-\Pi)e_x)\) because \(e_y\cdot e_x=0\). Hence \[u^{\mathsf T}G_Zu\ge m(\kappa+1)(1-2\epsilon^2)-m(\kappa-1){8\epsilon^4\over1-2\epsilon^2}\ge m(\kappa+1)\Bigl({7\over8}-{1\over28}\Bigr)\ge{4\over5}m(\kappa+1)\] for \(\epsilon\le1/4\). (6) \(\int\varphi^2\cosh(2\delta u)\,du\ge2c\int_s^{s'}\cosh(2\delta u)\,du\ge c(s'-s)e^{2|\delta|s}\), using both signs of \(u\) and \(\cosh t\ge e^{|t|}/2\). \(\square\)

What the trace sees. By (1) the trace premise of the density proof counts points and is blind to their distance from the line. All dependence on \(\delta\) sits in the second moment, through \(\kappa\).

Bandwidth threshold (a computation from the proposition, not a theorem about \(\zeta\)). Take \(\Lambda>4\), fixed \(\theta\) and bounded \(\eta\). For the density windows, \(\varphi^2(u)=\psi(u/\Lambda)\) for \(|u|\le\Lambda/2-1\) with \(\psi\ge\psi_{\min}=\cos(1/\sqrt2)>0.76\) there, so (6) with \(s=\Lambda/2-2\), \(s'=\Lambda/2-1\) gives \(\kappa\ge\psi_{\min}e^{\delta(\Lambda-4)}/\|\varphi\|_2^2\). By (4), a configuration with a solitary pair at distance \(\delta\) satisfies \[\delta\le{\log\|G_Z\|_{\mathrm{HS}}+\log(\|\varphi\|_2^2/\psi_{\min})\over\Lambda-4}.\] Insert a bound \(\|G_Z\|_{\mathrm{HS}}^2\le T^{1+\eta}\) at bandwidth \(\Lambda=\theta\log(T/2\pi)\), with \(\|\varphi\|_2^2=O(\Lambda)\): then \(\delta\le(1+\eta)/(2\theta)+O(\log\log T/\log T)\). The imported premise \(\|G\|_{\mathrm{HS}}^2=(R+o(1))N\) with \(N\sim(T/2\pi)\log T\) is the case \(\theta=1\), \(\eta=o(1)\): it yields \(\delta\le1/2+O(\log\log T/\log T)\), which is no restriction, since every nontrivial zero has \(\delta<1/2\). Equally, a zero with \(\delta\) close to \(1/2\) does not contradict the imported premise by this route. A restriction on a solitary zero would need \(\theta>1\): a second-moment bound for test functions longer than \(\log T\). The import contains none. Whether its prime-side argument extends past \(\theta=1\) is not asserted either way: it requires reading the source's second-moment proof, which this cycle, spent on the zero side, did not do; it is listed under Sub-ideas.

Gaps in applying this to the imported form. The proposition concerns the complete grid and any finite configuration. The import's \(G\) uses the zeros in a window, the cut grid and a window that is smoothed, and its premise is an upper bound for the cut form; (5) transfers the lower bound to the cut form under a tail hypothesis \(\epsilon\le1/4\) that is not verified here for the import's window. For a single zero the outcome at \(\theta=1\) is vacuous regardless of these gaps. For many zeros it is not known to be: an aggregate version of part (4), if one held with small loss, would turn the imported premise into a zero-density bound, so the gaps and the many-pair question matter at \(\theta=1\) as well (see the reviewer's suggestions).

Two-pair cancellation

Proposition (two-pair cancellation). prop:two-pair-cancellation. Let \(\varphi\) be the indicator of \(I\), so \(N_\varphi=\Lambda^2\), \(v_z^{\mathsf T}v_w/N_\varphi=\operatorname{sinc}((z-w)\Lambda/2)\) with \(\operatorname{sinc}x=\sin x/x\), and \(\kappa=\sinh b/b\) for \(b=\delta\Lambda\). For \(\delta>0\), real \(t\ne0\) and the configuration \(Z=\{\pm i\delta,\ t\pm i\delta\}\) with multiplicities one, \[\|G_Z\|_{\mathrm{HS}}^2=4+4\kappa^2+4\operatorname{sinc}^2(t\Lambda/2)+4\Re\operatorname{sinc}^2\bigl((t+2i\delta)\Lambda/2\bigr).\] At \(t=\pi/\Lambda\), with \(a=\pi/2\), \[\|G_Z\|_{\mathrm{HS}}^2=4+{16\over\pi^2}+4\kappa^2{3a^2b^2+a^4\over(b^2+a^2)^2}-{4(b^2-a^2)\over(b^2+a^2)^2}\le4+{16\over\pi^2}+{3\pi^2\kappa^2\over b^2}\quad(b\ge\pi/2).\] Each of the two pairs alone has \(\|G\|_{\mathrm{HS}}^2=2\kappa^2+2\). Hence there is no constant \(c>0\) with \(\|G_Z\|_{\mathrm{HS}}^2\ge c\sum_j(\kappa_j^2+1)\) for all configurations: the ratio tends to zero along \(b\to\infty\).

Proof. For any configuration, \(\|G_Z\|_{\mathrm{HS}}^2=\operatorname{tr}(G_Z^2)=N_\varphi^{-2}\sum_{z,w}m_zm_w(v_z^{\mathsf T}v_w)^2\), which here is \(\sum_{z,w\in Z}\operatorname{sinc}^2((z-w)\Lambda/2)\) by the lemma and \(\widehat{\varphi^2}(w)=2\sin(w\Lambda/2)/w\). Of the sixteen ordered pairs, four are diagonal and give \(1\) each. Four join a point to its conjugate, with difference \(\mp2i\delta\) and \(\operatorname{sinc}(\mp ib)=\sinh b/b=\kappa\). Four join points with equal imaginary parts, with difference \(\mp t\). The last four have the differences \(t+2i\delta\), \(-t-2i\delta\), \(t-2i\delta\), \(-t+2i\delta\), once each; \(\operatorname{sinc}\) is even and \(\operatorname{sinc}^2\) at conjugate arguments gives conjugate values, for a total of \(4\Re\operatorname{sinc}^2((t+2i\delta)\Lambda/2)\). At \(t\Lambda=\pi\): \(\operatorname{sinc}^2(\pi/2)=4/\pi^2\) and \(\sin(a+ib)=\cosh b\), so the last term is \(4\cosh^2b\,\Re(a+ib)^{-2}=-4\cosh^2b\,(b^2-a^2)/(b^2+a^2)^2\). With \(\cosh^2b=\sinh^2b+1\) and \(\sinh^2b=\kappa^2b^2\), \[4\kappa^2-{4\kappa^2b^2(b^2-a^2)\over(b^2+a^2)^2}=4\kappa^2{(b^2+a^2)^2-b^2(b^2-a^2)\over(b^2+a^2)^2}=4\kappa^2{3a^2b^2+a^4\over(b^2+a^2)^2},\] which gives the closed form. For the inequality, \((3a^2b^2+a^4)/(b^2+a^2)^2\le3a^2/b^2\) because \((3a^2b^2+a^4)b^2\le3a^2(b^2+a^2)^2\), and \(4\cdot3a^2=3\pi^2\); the last term of the closed form is nonpositive for \(b\ge a\). \(\square\)

What the cancellation means. The solitary hypothesis in part (4) cannot be replaced by a sum over pairs at a constant cost. Two off-line zeros at the same distance \(\delta\) and height difference \(\pi/\Lambda\) reduce the leading \(\kappa^2\) by the factor \(3\pi^2/(4b^2)\), which is a power of \(\log T\) when \(b=\delta\theta\log T\). Such a loss does not change the exponent in the bandwidth threshold. An interval of unit height holds up to order \(\log T\) zeros, and the loss for many close pairs is not computed here. That it could reach a power of \(T\) is an untested hypothesis and no decision rests on it; computing it is the first sub-idea and the proposed next step.

Numerical control

Nonvacuity. The script pair_atom_check.py computes, for the flat window on a grid cut at \(|k|\le4\cdot10^5\) and four pairs \((\Lambda,\delta)\) with \(b\) between \(2\) and \(6\) (\(\kappa\) between \(1.8\) and \(33.6\)), the quantities of the three statements themselves: the two Gram products, the two eigenvalues of a pair atom, the largest eigenvalue after adding forty random real points, and the two-pair norm at \(t\Lambda=\pi\) and, as a control without cancellation, at \(t\Lambda=2\pi\). A wrong sign, a wrong normalization or a missing cross term changes these numbers at relative order one, far above the truncation error of order \(10^{-5}\); the identities are not built into the code, which sums grid vectors. The solitary check tests only the direction of the inequality, not its sharpness (at \(\Lambda=8\) the largest eigenvalue is \(5.70\) against the bound \(2.81\)). All assertions pass (output): for example at \(\Lambda=20,\delta=0.3\) the atom eigenvalues are \(-32.61879\) and \(34.61883\) against \(\mp(\kappa\mp1)\) with \(\kappa=33.61886\), and the two-pair norm is \(0.185\) of \(4\kappa^2\) at \(t\Lambda=\pi\) and \(1.448\) of it at \(t\Lambda=2\pi\). The first version of the script also asserted that the \(\pi\) case lies below the \(2\pi\) case at \(b=2\); that comparison is asymptotic in \(b\), is not part of the proposition, failed at \(b=2\) and is now asserted only for \(b\ge4\). This control is not registered as a claim; the statements are proved above.

Sub-ideas

  • Compute the loss in \(\lambda_{\max}(G_Z)\) or \(\|G_Z\|_{\mathrm{HS}}\) for \(n\ge2\) off-axis pairs at close heights, as a function of \(n\) and \(b\), including an adaptively chosen test vector.
  • Read the density source's second-moment proof and locate each use of bandwidth at most \(\log T\).
  • Under the assumption that all points are real, bound \(\|G_Z\|_{\mathrm{HS}}^2\) at an arbitrary bandwidth by the local point counts, to see whether a second-moment premise at \(\theta>1\) is a consequence of the statement it is meant to prove.

Reviewer's suggestions, unverified

  • Paley–Wiener form: for real \(c\in\ell^2\), \(c\cdot v_z=H(z)\) with \(H\) of exponential type \(\Lambda/2\) and \(H(\bar z)=\overline{H(z)}\), so \(\lambda_{\max}(G_Z)\) is an extremal problem over such functions, with value governed by \(\sum_{\mathrm{real}}mH(\gamma)^2+2\sum_jm_j\Re H(z_j)^2\) against \(\|H\|_{L^2(\mathbb R)}^2\). Record. Searches for Paley-Wiener bandlimited, sinc kernel and interpolation vanishing test function returned no record on these objects.
  • Adaptive test vector: multiply a function from the chosen pair's two-dimensional span by a product of sines vanishing at the other off-axis points, spending part of the bandwidth; expected cost polynomial in \(b\) per pair killed, about \(b^{-2(n-1)}\) for fixed \(n\), choosing the pair of largest \(\delta\); far pairs and non-flat windows need care. Record. Searches for interpolation vanishing test function and Chebyshev extremal polynomial interval returned no record on these objects.
  • Matching example: heights \(j\pi/\Lambda\), equal \(\delta\), binomial multiplicities, giving an \((n-1)\)-th finite difference and an expected loss about \((C/b)^{2(n-1)}\); the multiplicity bound of order \(\log T\) per unit height allows only \(n\) of order \(\log\log T\) in this design, so it gives no power of \(T\). Whether simple zeros in general position can is the power-sum and large-values question. Record. Searches for Turan power sum, exponential sum close exponents lower bound and large sieve returned no record on these objects; lem:cube-pair-collision-valuation and prop:cube-cluster-order-loss treat clusters of close roots by divided differences in another setting and are queued as a lead.
  • Aggregate consequence at \(\theta=1\): an inequality \(\|G\|_{\mathrm{HS}}^2\ge T^{-o(1)}\sum_j\kappa_j^2\) would turn the imported premise into \(N(1/2+\delta,T)\le T^{1-2\delta+o(1)}\), the density hypothesis; so the passage from one pair to many has the strength of an open problem. Record. Searches for zeta zero, second moment Hilbert-Schmidt trace rank lower bound and large sieve returned only the imported thm:anthropic-critical-line-density and the three imported zero-free statements; no density estimate is recorded.
  • All-real case: then \(G_Z\succeq0\) and \(\|G\|_{\mathrm{HS}}^2\le\lambda_{\max}\operatorname{tr}G\), with \(\lambda_{\max}\) bounded by Schur's test from local zero counts, so the statement to be proved implies the \(\theta>1\) premise. Record. Searches for positivity kernel Bochner and Weil positivity compression returned no record on these objects; this is the third sub-idea.

Suggestion check. The first step of the last suggestion is verified: with all points real every atom is a rank-one projection, so \(G_Z\succeq0\), its eigenvalues \(\lambda_i\ge0\) satisfy \(\sum\lambda_i^2\le\lambda_{\max}\sum\lambda_i\), and \(\sum\lambda_i=\operatorname{tr}G_Z\). The Schur bound on \(\lambda_{\max}\) is not verified. The proposed next step, the many-pair loss, was this cycle's own open point before the review; the reviewer's constructions for it are leads, not results.

Verification gate. Dependencies re-read: the density import's zero-side construction, Poisson identity and analytic premises, from its full record. One fresh-context medium reviewer checked the lemma, the six parts of the proposition, the threshold computation and the two-pair identity and passed them with corrections, all incorporated: the import's window is even (the draft said otherwise); realness and \(\varphi\ne0\) added to the list of used hypotheses; \(\kappa\ge2\) suffices in part 5; \(0\le s<s'\) in part 6; \(\Lambda>4\), fixed \(\theta\) and bounded \(\eta\) in the threshold; the count of the last four terms reworded. The reviewer compared the quoted numbers with the stored output and ran nothing. Process audit. Findings, all acted on: the title and the Goal progress paragraph overstated the cancellation, whose proved loss is polylogarithmic and leaves the exponent unchanged (both reworded); the status line did not say what is refuted (now stated); the many-zero consequence at \(\theta=1\) was not asked (now in the gaps paragraph and the suggestions); the next step and the unread prime-side proof were named without being listed (now sub-ideas); the solitary control tests direction only (now said).

Process assessment. The draft contradicted the import record on the window's evenness although the record had been read, and it stated the two-pair cancellation as blocking the general case when only a polylogarithmic loss was proved; the reviewer caught both. The script's first run failed on an assertion that tested more than the proposition states. Prevention. For the overstated title and the statement contradicting a dependency, the committed checks are the reviewer's process audit and the per-entry Process audit paragraph, which caught both before the entry was appended; a mechanical check of a title's strength against a proof is not available. 11 October 2026 — Several off-line pairs: the Gram form's second moment is a mean square of the zero exponential sum; a norm bound with no loss for separated pairs and an unresolved loss for clusters

Working proofs in the abstract complete-grid model, the separated case and the real-configuration lemma for the flat window only; nothing is proved about actual zeta zeros. Many-pair bound. Produced by Claude Code (claude-opus-5-5, low effort).

Question. For off-line exclusion the missing implication is from a bound on the Gram form to the absence of every off-line zero; the previous entry had it only for a solitary pair and showed that two pairs can cancel. This cycle seeks a lower bound for the form with any number \(n\) of off-line pairs, with its loss stated exactly. The test ends with such a bound or with a configuration showing that none of the sought kind exists.

New mathematics. (1) An identity: for every configuration, \(\|G_Z\|_{\mathrm{HS}}^2\) is the mean square of the exponential sum \(S_Z(s)=\sum_{z\in Z}m_ze^{-isz}\) over \(|s|\le\Lambda\) against the weight \(\varphi^2*\varphi^2\). (2) When all points are real and at most \(M_{\mathrm{loc}}\) lie in any cell of length \(2\pi/\Lambda\), the flat-window form has \(\lambda_{\max}\le M_{\mathrm{loc}}(3+\tfrac2\pi(1+\ln K))\) for \(K\) cells. (3) A lower bound for the off-axis part \(B\) of the form in terms of the smallest eigenvalue \(\rho\) of a Gram matrix of exponentials, with \(\rho\ge3/5\) for the flat window when the off-line pairs are separated in height by \(8\) times their largest distance from the line. For clusters \(\rho\) can be very small and the bound is then empty; what actually happens for clusters is not determined.

Goal progress. Unchanged. The identity shows that a second-moment premise at bandwidth \(\Lambda\) is a mean-square bound for the zero sum \(\sum_\rho x^{\rho-1/2}\) with \(|\log x|\le\Lambda\). With the real-configuration bound, the statement to be proved implies that premise at every bandwidth (in the flat-window model, given a count of points per cell of length \(2\pi/\Lambda\)), and the separated-pair bound gives a partial converse: the premise at all large bandwidths excludes configurations all of whose off-line pairs are mutually separated and at distance at least \(1/\Lambda\) from the line. One cluster anywhere in the configuration voids that converse. My reading, on which no decision rests, is that in this model the premise beyond bandwidth \(\log T\) is close to a restatement of the goal for such configurations; no way to prove it is supplied. Clusters remain uncovered even conditionally.

General statement. prop:many-pair-norm-bound, for every bandwidth, every admissible window and every configuration with \(n\ge1\) off-axis pairs; its separated case for the flat window.

Review. The stand-in review of the previous cycle raised five objections. (1) Rebuilding the queue section after a finisher refusal without naming the cause: the cause was the start guidance, which did not say that the queue must be created after the entry; fixed in a separate framework commit that changes the guidance and the refusal text. (2) No statement of the missing implication at the start of the cycle and no mention of timing or registry search: wrong as to the record, where the Question paragraph states it, the timing session was started first and four registry searches preceded the lemmas; right that the chat did not say it, and this cycle states it at its start. (3) The next step was another computation in a restricted model with no general proposition: accepted; this cycle proves all-\(n\) statements first and uses the computation as a control, and the registry was searched before the proposition was drafted. (4) The corrections were not described: they are listed in that entry's Verification gate; one of them weakens a hypothesis (\(\kappa\ge2\) for \(\kappa\ge3\)) on the reviewer's own verification of the arithmetic. (5) The control was reported as passing without sizes: the sizes and what it distinguishes are in that entry's Nonvacuity paragraph; the chat summary omitted them.

Setting

As in the pair atom spectrum entry: \(\varphi\) real, even, nonzero, in \(L^2\), supported in \([-\Lambda/2,\Lambda/2]\); complete grid; \(N_\varphi=\Lambda\|\varphi\|_2^2\); \(G_Z=N_\varphi^{-1}\sum_{z\in Z}m_zv_zv_z^{\mathsf T}\) for a configuration \(Z\). Put \(g(w)=\widehat{\varphi^2}(w)/\|\varphi\|_2^2\), so that by the lemma there \(v_z^{\mathsf T}v_w/N_\varphi=g(z-w)\) and \(v_z^*v_w/N_\varphi=g(w-\bar z)\); \(g\) is even, \(g(\bar w)=\overline{g(w)}\), and \(g(-2i\delta)=\kappa(\delta)\). Let \(\omega=\varphi^2*\varphi^2\), an even nonnegative function supported in \([-\Lambda,\Lambda]\) with \(\int\omega=\|\varphi\|_2^4\) and \(\widehat\omega=(\widehat{\varphi^2})^2\). Write \(Z\cap\mathbb R\) for the real points, \(z_j=\gamma_j-i\delta_j\) (\(\delta_j>0\), \(1\le j\le n\), distinct) for the off-axis points below the axis, \(m_j\) for their multiplicities and \(\kappa_j=\kappa(\delta_j)\). Then \(G_Z=P+B\) with \[P={1\over N_\varphi}\sum_{z\in Z\cap\mathbb R}m_zv_zv_z^{\mathsf T}\succeq0,\qquad B={1\over N_\varphi}\sum_{j=1}^nm_j\bigl(v_{z_j}v_{z_j}^{\mathsf T}+v_{\bar z_j}v_{\bar z_j}^{\mathsf T}\bigr).\] For the flat window \(\varphi=1_{[-\Lambda/2,\Lambda/2]}\): \(g(w)=\operatorname{sinc}(w\Lambda/2)\), \(\omega(s)=\Lambda-|s|\) on \([-\Lambda,\Lambda]\), \(\kappa(\delta)=\sinh b/b\) with \(b=\delta\Lambda\).

Second-moment identity

Lemma (second-moment identity). lem:gram-second-moment-identity. For every configuration \(Z\), with \(S_Z(s)=\sum_{z\in Z}m_ze^{-isz}\), \[\|G_Z\|_{\mathrm{HS}}^2={1\over\|\varphi\|_2^4}\int_{-\Lambda}^{\Lambda}\omega(s)\,|S_Z(s)|^2\,ds,\qquad S_Z(s)=\sum_{z\in Z\cap\mathbb R}m_ze^{-is\gamma_z}+2\sum_{j=1}^nm_je^{-is\gamma_j}\cosh(\delta_js).\] The same holds for \(B\) with the second sum alone and for \(P\) with the first alone.

Proof. \(G_Z\) is real symmetric of finite rank, so \(\|G_Z\|_{\mathrm{HS}}^2=\operatorname{tr}(G_Z^2)=\sum_{z,w\in Z}m_zm_w\,g(z-w)^2\). Since \(g^2=\widehat\omega/\|\varphi\|_2^4\), \(g(z-w)^2=\|\varphi\|_2^{-4}\int\omega(s)e^{-isz}e^{isw}\,ds\), and summing gives \(\|\varphi\|_2^{-4}\int\omega(s)S_Z(s)S_Z(-s)\,ds\). For real \(s\), \(\overline{S_Z(s)}=\sum_zm_ze^{is\bar z}=\sum_zm_ze^{isz}=S_Z(-s)\), because \(Z\) and \(m\) are invariant under conjugation. So the integrand is \(\omega|S_Z|^2\). Pairing \(z_j\) with \(\bar z_j\) gives \(e^{-is\gamma_j}(e^{-s\delta_j}+e^{s\delta_j})\). The argument uses only conjugation invariance, so it applies to the sub-configurations defining \(B\) and \(P\). \(\square\)

Reading. With \(z_\rho=(\rho-1/2)/i\) and \(x=e^{s}\), \(e^{-isz_\rho}=x^{-(\rho-1/2)}\), and conjugation invariance makes \(|S_Z|\) even in \(s\). So the Hilbert–Schmidt norm of the Gram form at bandwidth \(\Lambda\) is a weighted mean square, over \(|\log x|\le\Lambda\), of the sum \(\sum_\rho m_\rho x^{\rho-1/2}\) over the zeros in the configuration (up to \(s\to-s\)). An off-line pair enters as \(2m\,e^{-is\gamma}\cosh(\delta s)\), of size \(e^{\delta|s|}\). The link of this zero sum to primes through the explicit formula is in the import; it is not re-derived here, and no statement about its size for \(x\) beyond \(T\) is made.

Real configurations

Lemma (real configuration bound). lem:real-configuration-bound. Flat window. Let all points of \(Z\) be real. Cut the line into cells of length \(2\pi/\Lambda\) starting at the smallest point; suppose each cell holds at most \(M_{\mathrm{loc}}\) points counted with multiplicity and the points occupy \(K\) consecutive cells. Then \[\lambda_{\max}(G_Z)\le M_{\mathrm{loc}}\Bigl(3+{2\over\pi}(1+\ln K)\Bigr),\qquad\|G_Z\|_{\mathrm{HS}}^2\le\lambda_{\max}(G_Z)\sum_{z\in Z}m_z.\] The second inequality holds for every window.

Proof. List the points with repetition as \(\gamma_1,\dots,\gamma_r\), \(r=\sum m_z\). Each \(v_{\gamma_i}/\sqrt{N_\varphi}\) is a real unit vector, so \(G_Z=EE^{\mathsf T}\) for the matrix \(E\) with these columns, and its nonzero eigenvalues are those of \(E^{\mathsf T}E=(g(\gamma_i-\gamma_k))_{i,k}\), a real symmetric positive semidefinite matrix. Its largest eigenvalue is at most the largest absolute row sum. Fix \(i\). The at most \(3M_{\mathrm{loc}}\) points in its own cell and the two neighbouring cells contribute at most \(1\) each, as \(|g|\le g(0)=1\) on the real line. A point in a cell at index distance \(k\ge2\) has \(|\gamma_i-\gamma|\ge(k-1)2\pi/\Lambda\), and \(|\operatorname{sinc}(t\Lambda/2)|\le2/(\Lambda|t|)\le1/(\pi(k-1))\). Summing over both sides, \(2M_{\mathrm{loc}}\sum_{k=2}^{K-1}1/(\pi(k-1))\le(2M_{\mathrm{loc}}/\pi)(1+\ln K)\). For the second inequality, the eigenvalues \(\lambda_i\ge0\) satisfy \(\sum\lambda_i^2\le\lambda_{\max}\sum\lambda_i\) and \(\sum\lambda_i=\operatorname{tr}G_Z=\sum m_z\) by the trace statement of solitary amplification. \(\square\)

Many-pair norm bound

Proposition (many-pair norm bound). prop:many-pair-norm-bound. In the setting above, with \(n\ge1\) off-axis pairs, let \(h_{jl}=g(z_j-\bar z_l)\), so \(h_{jj}=\kappa_j\) and \(h_{lj}=\overline{h_{jl}}\), and \[R_{jl}={h_{jl}^2\over\kappa_j\kappa_l},\qquad\rho=\lambda_{\min}(R).\]

  1. Reduction to the off-axis part. \(\|G_Z\|_{\mathrm{HS}}\ge\|B\|_{\mathrm{HS}}-\|P\|_{\mathrm{HS}}\), and \(\lambda_{\max}(G_Z)\ge\lambda_{\max}(B)\ge\|B\|_{\mathrm{HS}}/(2n)^{3/2}\).
  2. Gram of exponentials. \(R\) is Hermitian positive semidefinite with unit diagonal, so \(0\le\rho\le1\); it is the Gram matrix, in \(L^2(\omega\,ds)\) on \([-\Lambda,\Lambda]\), of the normalized exponentials \(e^{-isz_j}\).
  3. Norm identity. \(\|B\|_{\mathrm{HS}}^2=2\sum_{j,l}m_jm_lh_{jl}^2+2\Re\sum_{j,l}m_jm_l\,g(z_j-z_l)^2\), and the first sum equals \(c^{\mathsf T}Rc\) for the real vector \(c=(m_j\kappa_j)_j\).
  4. Lower bound. \(\|B\|_{\mathrm{HS}}^2\ge2\rho\sum_jm_j^2\kappa_j^2-\sum_{j,l}m_jm_l\bigl(1+\kappa(\delta_j-\delta_l)\bigr)\).
  5. Separated pairs, flat window. Suppose \(\delta_j\Lambda\ge1\) and \(\delta_j\le\Delta\) for all \(j\), and \(|\gamma_j-\gamma_l|\ge\tau\) for \(j\ne l\), with \(\tau\ge8\Delta\). Then \(|R_{jl}|\le\tfrac74\bigl(1+(\gamma_j-\gamma_l)^2/(\delta_j+\delta_l)^2\bigr)^{-1}\) for \(j\ne l\) and \(\rho\ge3/5\). If moreover \(\kappa_*=\max_j\kappa_j\ge5M^2+1\) with \(M=\sum_jm_j\), then \(\|B\|_{\mathrm{HS}}\ge\kappa_*\).

Proof. (1) The first inequality is the triangle inequality for \(G_Z=P+B\). Since \(P\succeq0\), \(\lambda_{\max}(P+B)\ge\lambda_{\max}(B)\). \(B\) is real symmetric of rank at most \(2n\) with \(\operatorname{tr}B=2\sum_jm_j>0\) by the trace statement. If its operator norm is attained at a positive eigenvalue, \(\lambda_{\max}(B)=\|B\|_{\mathrm{op}}\). Otherwise it is attained at an eigenvalue \(-\mu<0\); as the eigenvalues sum to a positive number, the positive ones sum to more than \(\mu\), and there are fewer than \(2n\) of them, so \(\lambda_{\max}(B)\ge\mu/(2n)\). In both cases \(\lambda_{\max}(B)\ge\|B\|_{\mathrm{op}}/(2n)\ge\|B\|_{\mathrm{HS}}/(2n)^{3/2}\), the last step because \(\|B\|_{\mathrm{HS}}^2\le\operatorname{rank}(B)\|B\|_{\mathrm{op}}^2\). (2) With \(a_j=v_{z_j}/|v_{z_j}|\) and the inner product \(\langle x,y\rangle=y^*x\) on \(\ell^2\otimes\ell^2\), \(\langle a_j\otimes a_j,a_l\otimes a_l\rangle=(a_l^*a_j)^2=(v_{z_l}^*v_{z_j})^2/(|v_{z_j}|^2|v_{z_l}|^2)=h_{jl}^2/(\kappa_j\kappa_l)\), using \(v_{z_l}^*v_{z_j}=N_\varphi g(z_j-\bar z_l)\) and \(|v_{z_j}|^2=\kappa_jN_\varphi\). A Gram matrix is Hermitian positive semidefinite, and the diagonal is \(1\). Also \(h_{jl}^2=\|\varphi\|_2^{-4}\widehat\omega(z_j-\bar z_l)=\|\varphi\|_2^{-4}\int\omega(s)e^{-isz_j}\overline{e^{-isz_l}}\,ds\), which is the stated Gram matrix after normalization. (3) Expand \(\operatorname{tr}(B^2)=N_\varphi^{-2}\sum m_zm_w(v_z^{\mathsf T}v_w)^2\) over \(z,w\in\{z_j\}\cup\{\bar z_j\}\). The pairs \((z_j,z_l)\) give \(g(z_j-z_l)^2\) and the pairs \((\bar z_j,\bar z_l)\) the conjugates. For \((\bar z_j,z_l)\), \(v_{\bar z_j}^{\mathsf T}v_{z_l}=v_{z_j}^*v_{z_l}=N_\varphi h_{lj}\), and for \((z_j,\bar z_l)\) the conjugate, \(N_\varphi h_{jl}\); summing over \(j,l\) both give \(\sum m_jm_lh_{jl}^2\). That sum is \(\sum_{j,l}(m_j\kappa_j)(m_l\kappa_l)R_{jl}\). (4) By (2), \(c^{\mathsf T}Rc\ge\rho|c|^2\). For the other sum, \(|g(z_j-z_l)|\le\|\varphi\|_2^{-2}\int\varphi^2e^{-u(\delta_j-\delta_l)}\,du=\kappa((\delta_j-\delta_l)/2)\) by evenness, and by Cauchy–Schwarz \(\kappa(d/2)^2\le\|\varphi\|_2^{-2}\int\varphi^2\cosh^2(du)\,du=(1+\kappa(d))/2\); so \(2\Re\sum\ge-\sum m_jm_l(1+\kappa(\delta_j-\delta_l))\). (5) Here \(h_{jl}=\operatorname{sinc}((t-is)\Lambda/2)\) with \(t=\gamma_j-\gamma_l\), \(s=\delta_j+\delta_l\). From \(|\sin(x+iy)|^2=\sin^2x+\sinh^2y\le\cosh^2y\), with \(b_j=\delta_j\Lambda\), \[|R_{jl}|\le{\cosh^2((b_j+b_l)/2)\over\sinh b_j\sinh b_l}\cdot{4b_jb_l\over t^2\Lambda^2+(b_j+b_l)^2}.\] The first factor equals \(\tfrac12\bigl(1/(\sinh b_j\sinh b_l)+\coth b_j\coth b_l+1\bigr)\), which decreases in \(b_j,b_l\) and at \(b_j=b_l=1\) is \(1.724<7/4\). The second is at most \(\bigl(1+t^2\Lambda^2/(b_j+b_l)^2\bigr)^{-1}\) because \(4b_jb_l\le(b_j+b_l)^2\). This is the entry bound; it gives \(|R_{jl}|\le\tfrac74(2\Delta)^2/t^2\). Ordering the heights, the \(k\)-th neighbour on either side is at distance at least \(k\tau\), so \(\sum_{l\ne j}|R_{jl}|\le2\cdot7\Delta^2\tau^{-2}\pi^2/6=(7\pi^2/3)(\Delta/\tau)^2\le0.36\) for \(\tau\ge8\Delta\), and Gershgorin's theorem gives \(\rho\ge0.64\). Finally \(\kappa\) is even and increasing in \(|\delta|\), so \(\kappa(\delta_j-\delta_l)\le\kappa_*\), and (4) gives \(\|B\|_{\mathrm{HS}}^2\ge\tfrac65\kappa_*^2-M^2(1+\kappa_*)\ge\kappa_*^2\) when \(\tfrac15\kappa_*^2\ge M^2(1+\kappa_*)\), which holds for \(\kappa_*\ge5M^2+1\). \(\square\)

Consistency with the two-pair cancellation. For two pairs with equal \(\delta\) at height difference \(\pi/\Lambda\), \(|R_{12}|=\coth^2b\cdot b^2/(a^2+b^2)\) with \(a=\pi/2\), so \(\rho=1-|R_{12}|\approx a^2/b^2\), and (4) gives \(\|B\|_{\mathrm{HS}}^2\ge\pi^2\kappa^2/b^2-8\) to leading order, below the exact leading term \(3\pi^2\kappa^2/b^2\) of the earlier proposition, as it must be.

What the proposition covers (a computation from it, not a theorem about \(\zeta\)). In the flat-window model at bandwidth \(\Lambda=\theta\log(T/2\pi)\), take a configuration all of whose off-axis pairs are separated as in (5) and have \(\delta_j\Lambda\ge1\) (with \(\Delta<1/2\), height separation \(4\) suffices) and whose real points obey the local count of the real configuration bound. If \(\|G_Z\|_{\mathrm{HS}}^2\le T^{1+\eta}\), then by (1), (5) and the lemma \(\kappa_*\le\max\bigl(5M^2+1,\ T^{(1+\eta)/2}+\|P\|_{\mathrm{HS}}\bigr)\). With \(M\le T^A\) and the number of real points at most a fixed power of \(T\) this bounds the largest \(\delta\) by roughly \(\max(2A,(1+\eta)/2)/\theta\): the constant depends on \(A\) through the threshold \(5M^2+1\), so the absence of loss holds for the norm and not for this threshold. The bound tends to zero as \(\theta\to\infty\). Conversely the lemma shows that a configuration of real points with the local count has \(\|G_Z\|_{\mathrm{HS}}^2\) at most the number of points times \(M_{\mathrm{loc}}(3+\tfrac2\pi(1+\ln K))\) at every bandwidth, where \(M_{\mathrm{loc}}\) is counted in cells whose length depends on the bandwidth. So for such configurations the premise at all bandwidths and the absence of off-axis points are close to equivalent in the flat-window complete-grid model.

What it does not cover. When pairs are closer in height than their distance from the line, \(\rho\) is small and (4) is empty. The control below has random clusters of five and eight pairs with \(\rho\) between \(10^{-13}\) and \(10^{-5}\), for which the true \(\|B\|_{\mathrm{HS}}^2\) is nevertheless between \(0.43\) and \(1.55\) times \(2\sum m_j^2\kappa_j^2\); equally spaced pairs at step \(\pi/\Lambda\) give ratios between \(0.09\) and \(0.24\) for two to six pairs. So \(\rho\), which is a worst case over all coefficient vectors, is far from what happens for the actual vector \(c=(m_j\kappa_j)\) with its positive entries in these samples. These are ten sampled configurations at one bandwidth, and the resonant ones at the single value \(b=8.4\): they cannot speak to the dependence on \(b\) or \(n\). Random clusters have generic phases, so they separate a weak bound from a weak form only for generic phases and test nothing adversarial. For adversarial clusters the record already points the other way: the previous entry's unverified suggestion (binomial multiplicities, loss about \((C/b)^{2(n-1)}\)) and this cycle's reviewer's family of \(2^k\) simple points (below, unverified beyond its first step) both indicate a loss exceeding any fixed power of \(b\) as \(n\) grows. So the open question is not whether a fixed power of \(b\) bounds the loss, but how the loss depends on \(n\), and whether it survives a change of bandwidth, since each such cancellation is a resonance at one \(\Lambda\). By the identity this is a question about the mean square of \(\sum_jm_je^{-is\gamma_j}\cosh(\delta_js)\) near \(|s|=\Lambda\).

Numerical control

Nonvacuity. The script many_pair_check.py uses the flat window at \(\Lambda=24\) on a grid cut at \(|k|\le5\cdot10^4\) and 22 configurations: twelve random clusters (\(n=2,3,5,8\), heights within \(0.4\), \(\delta\in[0.2,0.45]\), multiplicities \(1\) or \(2\), thirty random real points), four equally spaced equal-\(\delta\) clusters at step \(\pi/\Lambda\), and six separated configurations (\(n=2,4,8\), gaps in \([4,6]\), forty real points). For each it computes from grid vectors the spectra of \(B\) and \(G\) and both Hilbert–Schmidt norms, and compares them with the closed forms: the norm identity (3), the second-moment identity by a trapezoid rule on \(4\cdot10^5\) intervals, the bound (4), the chain in (1), the row-sum bound of the real lemma, and in the separated cases the entry bound, \(\rho\ge3/5\) and, where \(\kappa_*\ge5M^2+1\), the consequence \(\lambda_{\max}(G)\ge\kappa_*/(2n)^{3/2}\) (the script does not assert \(\|B\|_{\mathrm{HS}}\ge\kappa_*\) itself). The command was run with the grid option at \(5\cdot10^4\), now the script's default. The identities are equalities between independently computed numbers, where an error in a sign, a factor \(2\) or a missing cross term shows at relative order one against a tolerance of \(5\cdot10^{-3}\); for instance at one cluster of three the grid value is \(9512488\) and the quadrature \(9512820\). The inequalities are tested for direction only: the separated cases have \(\rho\) between \(0.936\) and \(0.988\), well above \(3/5\), and the real row-sum bound is four to nine times the observed \(\lambda_{\max}(P)\). All checks pass (output). Two earlier runs did not complete: one stopped on an empty list of real points in the script, one reached the 60-second limit at the longer grid. The control is not registered as a claim.

Sub-ideas

  • Verify the reviewer's family of \(2^k\) simple points with subset-sum heights and determine the loss of \(\int\omega(s)\,|\sum_jm_je^{-is\gamma_j}\cosh(\delta_js)|^2\,ds\) against \(\sum_jm_j^2\kappa_j^2\) as a function of \(n\) and \(b\), between the exponents \(2\log_2n\) and \(2(n-1)\).
  • Decide whether for every configuration with equal \(\delta\) some bandwidth in \([\Lambda_0,2\Lambda_0]\) has \(\|B\|_{\mathrm{HS}}^2\ge c\sum_jm_j^2\kappa_j^2\), by a mean value over \(s\), and what unequal \(\delta\) changes.
  • Try the adaptive test vector of the previous entry's suggestions on a cluster, for \(\lambda_{\max}\) directly.

Follow-up. Continuing: the check asked for the loss with \(n\ge2\) pairs as a function of \(n\) and \(b\). It is answered for configurations all of whose pairs are separated and have \(\delta_j\Lambda\ge1\) (no loss in \(b\) for the norm when \(\kappa_*\ge5M^2+1\), a factor \((2n)^{3/2}\) when passing to \(\lambda_{\max}\)) and reduced, for clusters, to the mean square of an explicit exponential sum, where the dependence on \(n\) is open. The adaptive test vector was not tried, because the second-moment identity made the norm question exact and took the cycle; it is now a sub-idea. The item still bears on off-line exclusion through clusters. Next attempt: the first two sub-ideas above.

Reviewer's suggestions, unverified

  • A family of simple points with a loss beyond every fixed power of \(b\): flat window, equal \(\delta\), \(n=2^k\) points at heights \(\sum_{i\in S}\alpha_i\) over subsets \(S\), \(\alpha_i=q_i\pi/\Lambda\) with \(q_i\) odd and distinct subset sums (for instance \(q_i=3^{i-1}\)). Then \(\sum_Se^{-is\gamma_S}=\prod_i(1+e^{-is\alpha_i})\) vanishes to order \(k\) at \(s=\Lambda\), and the reviewer's estimate gives a ratio about \((2k+1)!\,2^{-k}\prod_i(q_i\pi/2b)^2\), of order \(b^{-2\log_2n}\); with a local count of order \(\log T\) per unit height, \(k\) is at most about \(\log\log T\) and the loss about \(\exp(-c(\log\log T)^2)\), short of a power of \(T\). Record. Searches for Turan power sum and exponential sum close exponents lower bound returned no record on these objects; prop:two-pair-cancellation is the case \(k=1\), and the previous entry's binomial suggestion is the same construction with multiplicities.
  • Lower bound side: for equal \(\delta\), the Turán–Nazarov lemma on a subinterval of length about \(1/\delta\) at the end should give a loss of at most \((c/b)^{2(n-1)}\); the weighted \(L^2\) form and unequal \(\delta\) are unchecked. Record. Searches for Turan power sum and Riesz basis exponentials lower bound returned no record on these objects.
  • Use the freedom in the bandwidth: each cancellation is a resonance at one \(\Lambda\), and the mean of \(|\sum_je^{-is\gamma_j}|^2\) over \(s\in[\Lambda_0,2\Lambda_0]\) is about \(n\) when the gaps exceed \(1/\Lambda_0\), which suggests that some bandwidth in that range has no loss, for equal \(\delta\). Record. Searches for Hilbert inequality Montgomery Vaughan and large sieve returned no record on these objects.

Suggestion check. The first step of the first suggestion is verified: for odd \(q\), \(1+e^{-i\Lambda q\pi/\Lambda}=1+e^{-iq\pi}=0\), so each factor of the product vanishes at \(s=\Lambda\) and the product to order \(k\); and \(k=1\) with \(q_1=1\) is the two-pair configuration, whose recorded ratio \(3\pi^2/(4b^2)\) is \(0.105\) at \(b=8.4\) against \(0.099\) in the control. The norm estimate for \(k\ge2\) is not verified; verifying it is the first sub-idea and is not relied on.

Verification gate. Dependencies re-read: the pair atom spectrum lemma and the trace statement of solitary amplification, from their full record. One fresh-context medium reviewer checked both lemmas and the five parts of the proposition step by step and found no mathematical error; it ran nothing and compared the quoted numbers with the stored output. Corrections, all incorporated: the range of the row-sum ratio (four to nine, not five to six); the control asserts the \(\lambda_{\max}\) consequence, not the final norm inequality; the separated case requires every off-axis pair of the configuration to be separated and to have \(\delta_j\Lambda\ge1\); the bound on \(\delta\) depends on the exponent of \(M\); the real lemma and the converse are flat-window statements; a sign remark in the reading. 11 October 2026 — Where the density proof uses bandwidth at most one: read from its Lean statements; the off-diagonal prime bound is the single place, and at its strength no bandwidth constrains a zero

Source reading, no new result; statements read in the retained Lean files and their docstrings, not in the paper's text. Bandwidth reading. Produced by Claude Code (claude-opus-5-5, low effort).

Chosen. The queue's head is the many-pair loss; this entry takes the second item, the reading of the density source, because it bears on off-line exclusion through the first missing part (a second-moment bound beyond bandwidth \(\log T\)) and its outcome decides whether the cluster question is worth further cycles on this route, as the proposed next step said.

Question. The missing implication is unchanged: from an available estimate to the exclusion of one off-line zero. The Gram model needs a second-moment bound at bandwidth \(\theta\log T\), \(\theta>1\). This cycle reads, in the source, where bandwidth at most one is assumed and what the proof's estimates would give beyond it. The test ends with the located statements and the size of the term that fails.

New mathematics. None. The reading and one bookkeeping computation from prop:solitary-amplification are below.

Goal progress. Unchanged. The source restricts its exponent to \(0<\lambda\le1\), and the one estimate whose error grows with the bandwidth is the Montgomery–Vaughan bound for the off-diagonal prime terms, of size \(L^2X\) with \(X=(T/2\pi)^\lambda\). Carried past \(\lambda=1\) at that strength, it would bound the second moment by about \(T^{\theta}\), and the solitary bound then gives \(\delta\le1/2+o(1)\) at every bandwidth: nothing. A restriction on a zero by this route needs a power saving over that off-diagonal bound, which the source does not contain. Bearing. At the goal's parameters this entry gives nothing: it records which estimate is missing and how strong it would have to be. The cycle was spent on it because the previous entry's next step made the choice between the cluster question and a change of method depend on this reading.

General statement. prop:solitary-amplification is the all-parameter statement used; the bookkeeping below evaluates it for every \(\theta\ge1\) under a stated hypothetical bound.

What was read

All statements are from the retained project under the density import, files under its Zeta23 directory. Docstrings there quote the paper's labels and sentences; the paper's own text was not opened in this cycle.

  • Parameters (Defs.lean). The fixed parameters are a taper profile, an exponent \(\lambda\) and a ramp width \(w\); \(l=\log(T/2\pi)\), \(L=\lambda l\), \(X=e^{L}=(T/2\pi)^\lambda\), grid step \(2\pi/L\). The structure of standing assumptions requires \(0<\lambda\le1\) and \(w\ge1\).
  • Relative error (Defs.lean, PrimeSideB.lean). \(\mathcal E_T=w/L+(l^2+X)\log l/(Tl)+T^{\lambda/2-1}\), and the theorem that \(\mathcal E_T\to0\) is stated for every fixed \(0<\lambda\le1\); its docstring quotes the paper: \(\mathcal E_T\ll_\lambda w/L+T^{\lambda-1}\log l\) for \(\lambda<1\) and \(\ll w/L+\log l/l\) for \(\lambda=1\). The proof uses the eventual inequality \(X\le T\), which is derived from \(\lambda\le1\).
  • Second trace (PrimeSideB.lean). First form: \(\operatorname{tr}\tilde G^2=2\pi bL\int_T^{2T}\mu^2+(T/\pi)\sum_{n\le X}\Lambda(n)^2n^{-1}g(\log n)+O\bigl(Ll\log l\,(l^2+X)\bigr)\), in a regime that includes \(L\le l\) and \(X\le T\).
  • Prime diagonal and off-diagonal (PrimeSideB/PP.lean, PPOffDiag.lean). The proposition quoted from the paper's section 5.4: \(\mathcal M[P_X,P_X]=(T/\pi)\sum_{n\le X}\Lambda(n)^2n^{-1}g(\log n)+O(L^2X)\), with hypotheses a Chebyshev–Mertens input, the Montgomery–Vaughan inequality with any constant, and \(0<\lambda\le1\). The off-diagonal term is bounded, for every \(X\) and \(T\ge0\), by \(16C(\int\Phi^2)\sum_{n\le X}\Lambda(n)^2\); this theorem has no hypothesis on \(\lambda\), and with \(\sum_{n\le X}\Lambda(n)^2\ll XL\) and \(\int\Phi^2\le2\pi L\) it is the \(O(L^2X)\).
  • Montgomery–Vaughan (MV.lean). The weighted Hilbert inequality \(|\sum_{r\ne s}x_r\bar z_s/(\lambda_r-\lambda_s)|\le C(\sum|x_r|^2/\delta_r)^{1/2}(\sum|z_r|^2/\delta_r)^{1/2}\), applied with frequencies \(\log n\).
  • The source's own ceiling at bandwidth one (README, PairCeiling/Ceiling.lean, CeilingLaw256.lean). For a certificate \((c_0,r)\) of the kind used for the density theorem that is valid at a configuration whose form-factor measure has masses \(s_j\) at \(j/N\) and simple fraction \(p\): \(c_0+\int_0^1r(x)x\,dx\le p+|r(1)||D(1)|+|r'(1)||E(1)|+\sup|E|\int_0^1|r''|\). At an explicit 256-periodic law, under one displayed numeric hypothesis on its form factor that is not checked in the kernel, the bound reads \(p+0.82395317\,|r(1)|+2.5431316\cdot10^{-6}(|r'(1)|+\int_0^1|r''|)\); the README gives the resulting ceiling for the certified proportion of simple zeros as \(0.6818287\) plus the small terms. No local build or replay was made, as before.

What the reading says about bandwidth

The main terms of the second trace are of order \(TLl^2\) and \(TL^3\). The error is \(Ll\log l\,(l^2+X)\), so relative to the main term it is about \(X\log l/(Tl)\). At \(\lambda=1\) this is \(\log l/l\), which still tends to zero; for \(\lambda>1\) it is \(T^{\lambda-1}\log l/l\), which does not. The term responsible is the off-diagonal prime sum, bounded by the Montgomery–Vaughan inequality with frequencies \(\log n\) and gap weights \(2n\) in the docstring's form: its size \(L^2X\) is a bound and no asymptotic for it is available in the source. So \(\lambda\le1\) is used in exactly one essential way, the comparison \(X\le T\) between that bound and the diagonal. The other terms of \(\mathcal E_T\) were not examined for \(\lambda>1\).

Bookkeeping (hypothetical, not a statement about \(\zeta\)). Suppose that for some \(\theta\ge1\) the Gram form at bandwidth \(\theta\log(T/2\pi)\) satisfied \(\|G\|_{\mathrm{HS}}^2\le T^{\theta-\varepsilon+o(1)}\) with \(0\le\varepsilon\le\theta-1\); \(\varepsilon=0\) is the size of the off-diagonal bound \(L^2X\) with \(X=(T/2\pi)^\theta\). Whether the source's remaining estimates extend to \(\theta>1\) so as to give even \(\varepsilon=0\) is not checked. In the complete-grid model, the bandwidth threshold of the pair atom spectrum entry with \(1+\eta=\theta-\varepsilon\) gives, for a solitary off-line pair, \[\delta\le{\theta-\varepsilon\over2\theta}+o(1)={1\over2}-{\varepsilon\over2\theta}+o(1).\] At \(\varepsilon=0\) this is \(\delta\le1/2+o(1)\) for every \(\theta\): the trivial off-diagonal bound constrains no zero at any bandwidth. A zero-free region \(\delta\le1/2-c\) by this route needs a saving \(\varepsilon=2c\theta\), a fixed positive power of \(X\) over Montgomery–Vaughan in the off-diagonal prime sum; the goal needs \(\varepsilon=\theta-1\), the full saving, at all large \(\theta\). By the many-pair bound entry, in the flat-window model real configurations with a local count do satisfy the full saving, so the saving is a consequence of the goal there.

Consistency with the source's ceiling. The source's own statement is that certificates at bandwidth one certify at most about \(0.68\) of the zeros as simple, at its model law and under its numeric hypothesis. That concerns proportions and not single zeros, but it agrees in kind with the previous two entries: bandwidth-one information does not exclude exceptional zeros.

What this decides. On this route the estimate that would have to be supplied is a power saving in an off-diagonal sum over prime powers \(n\ne m\le T^\theta\) with \(\theta>1\), where the diagonal no longer dominates. Nothing in the two imports is recorded here as giving one by this route. The seven-eighths source claims a zero-free half-plane by a different mechanism, and by the bookkeeping a half-plane \(\delta\le3/8\) corresponds to a saving \(\varepsilon=\theta/4\); how that source obtains its exponent is therefore the more informative thing to read next, before more cycles go to clusters in the Gram model.

Follow-up. Developed: the check asked to read the density source's second-moment proof and locate each use of bandwidth at most \(\log T\). The parameter restriction, the error term, the second-trace formula and the off-diagonal bound are located and quoted above, and the essential use is the comparison \(X\le T\) for the off-diagonal term. Sources read. Read in their text, in the retained Lean project: the parameter structure and the definitions of \(L\), \(X\) and \(\mathcal E_T\) in Defs.lean; the statement and docstring that \(\mathcal E_T\to0\), the regime lemma and the first form of the second trace in PrimeSideB.lean; the statement of the prime-diagonal proposition in PrimeSideB/PP.lean and of the off-diagonal bound with its header in PrimeSideB/PPOffDiag.lean; the header of MV.lean; the ceiling paragraph of README.md and the statements of the ceiling theorems in PairCeiling/Ceiling.lean and CeilingLaw256.lean. Not read: the proofs, the remaining terms of \(\mathcal E_T\) for \(\lambda>1\), and the paper's prose, which the docstrings quote.

Verification gate. No new lemma, theorem, rule or encoding is stated, so no reviewer was used in this cycle; the bookkeeping is the registered threshold computation with \(1+\eta\) replaced by \(\theta-\varepsilon\). The quoted statements were copied from the files named under Sources read. The route review, due after three more research entries, must test the reading's conclusion (that a power saving in the off-diagonal sum is what the route needs) against the sources.

Process assessment. The source's Lean tree answered the question in a few targeted reads because its docstrings carry the paper's labels; the earlier two cycles could have opened with this reading and reached the threshold statement sooner. The claim-registry check after each rebase now takes 8 to 15 seconds against its 5-second limit while other sessions share the machine and the branch is far ahead of the published base. Prevention. For the order of work, the existing rule to read the named sources first applies and was followed late; no new rule is needed. The slow check is reported, not a mistake of this cycle: it is queued as a build item in this notebook's queue.

11 October 2026 — How the seven-eighths source excludes zeros: its continuation criterion read with its proof; the text read does not say what stops the exponent

Source reading, no new result; the criterion and its proof read in the retained TeX, the long estimates behind its hypotheses not read. Criterion reading. Produced by Claude Code (claude-opus-5-5, low effort).

Question. The missing implication for off-line exclusion is an estimate that rules out one zero with \(\Re\rho>1/2\). The bandwidth reading found that the Gram route needs a power saving in an off-diagonal prime sum. This cycle reads how the only source in the workspace that claims a zero-free half-plane obtains it: which statement turns estimates into exclusion, and where the exponent \(7/8\) is fixed. The test ends with the criterion stated from the text and either the limiting estimate named from the text or the finding that the text read does not name one.

New mathematics. None. One proposition of the source was read with its complete proof and checked step by step; it is restated below.

Goal progress. Unchanged. The source's exclusion step is a continuation criterion for the reciprocal of the \(L\)-function, applied twice: first with boundary \(11/12\), then, assuming the supremum \(\beta_*\) of real parts of zeros lies in \((7/8,11/12]\), with boundary \(7/8\). The second stage is built on the first stage's conclusion. The parts read fix the exponents by an explicit choice of scales and do not state which inequality prevents a lower boundary. Bearing. At the goal's parameters this entry gives nothing: it identifies the form an exclusion argument takes in the source and leaves open whether that form can reach below \(7/8\). The cycle was spent on it because the previous entry made the choice of route depend on it.

General statement. thm:openai-seven-eighths-half-plane is the registered all-parameter statement this reading concerns; the criterion below is its reduction step, valid for every boundary \(\sigma_0\in(1/2,1)\).

The criterion, from the text

Source: the retained TeX of the seven-eighths paper, its section "From a common signal to a zero-free half-plane". There \(F=\mathbb Q(\sqrt{-3})\), and \(\beta_*\) is the supremum of \(1/2\) and of the real parts of zeros in \(1/2\le\Re\rho\le1\) of \(L_F(s,\eta)\) over all primitive finite-order Hecke characters \(\eta\); a superscript \(\mathcal S\) deletes the Euler factors at a finite set of primes.

Proposition (continuation from a common signal; source statement). Fix \(\sigma_0\in(1/2,1)\), suppose \(\Delta_0=\beta_*-\sigma_0>0\), and let \(C(s)=s+c\) with real \(c\). Suppose \(0<\omega<\Delta_0\) and \(\sigma>0\) can be chosen independently of the character, and that for every primitive \(\eta\) there are a finite set \(\mathcal S\), a holomorphic \(H_\eta\) on \(\Re s>\sigma_0\) with \(\sup|H_\eta-1|\le1/2\), and a function \(J_\eta(Z)\) for large \(Z\) such that, with \[f_\eta(Z)={1\over2\pi i}\int_{\Re s=2}Z^{C(s)}e^{(s-5/6)^2}{H_\eta(s)\over L_F^{\mathcal S}(s,\eta)}\,ds,\] one has \(|J_\eta(Z)|\ll_\eta Z^{C(\sigma_0)+\omega}\) and \(|J_\eta(Z)-f_\eta(Z)|\ll_\eta Z^{C(\beta_*)-\sigma}\) as \(Z\to\infty\). Then these assumptions contradict \(\beta_*>\sigma_0\).

Its proof, as read. With \(\epsilon_*=\min(\Delta_0-\omega,\sigma)\), the two bounds give \(|f_\eta(Z)|\ll Z^{C(\beta_*)-\epsilon_*}\) for large \(Z\), since \(C(\sigma_0)+\omega=C(\beta_*)-(\Delta_0-\omega)\). Moving the contour to the right, where the reciprocal Euler product is bounded and the Gaussian factor gives decay, shows \(|f_\eta(Z)|\ll_BZ^{B+c}\) for \(Z\le1\) and every \(B\). So the Mellin transform \(F_\eta(s)=\int_0^\infty f_\eta(Z)Z^{-C(s)}\,dZ/Z\) is holomorphic on \(\Re s>\beta_*-\epsilon_*\). Fourier inversion on the line \(\Re s=2\) and the identity theorem give \(F_\eta(s)=e^{(s-5/6)^2}H_\eta(s)/L_F^{\mathcal S}(s,\eta)\) on \(\Re s>1\). Since \(|H_\eta|\ge1/2\), \(e^{-(s-5/6)^2}F_\eta/H_\eta\) continues the reciprocal holomorphically to \(\Re s>\beta_*-\epsilon_*\), where \(\beta_*-\epsilon_*>\sigma_0\ge1/2\). As \(\epsilon_*\) does not depend on \(\eta\), the definition of the supremum gives a character with a zero in that half-plane, a pole of the reciprocal: contradiction. I checked each step; the only inputs are the two bounds, the bound on \(H_\eta\) and the absolute convergence of the Euler product for \(\Re s>1\).

How it is applied, from the text. The first stage takes \(\sigma_0=11/12\), \(C(s)=s-2/3\), \(C(\sigma_0)=1/4\), with both averaging scales equal to \(Z^{1/2}\), and concludes \(\beta_*\le11/12\) for the Hecke family; a transfer gives Dirichlet \(L\)-functions and \(\zeta\). The second stage starts from that conclusion: it supposes \(\beta_*>7/8\), so \(0<\Delta=\beta_*-7/8\le1/24\) and \(\kappa=2\beta_*-1=3/4+2\Delta\le5/6\), takes \(C(s)=s-11/16\), \(C(7/8)=3/16\), and fixes the scales \(b=1/8\), \(h=13/16\), \(\ell=1/6\), \(l_x=17/48\), \(l_y=23/48\), \(X=Z^{l_x}\), \(Y=Z^{l_y}\). Its low-side target is \(|J(Z)|\ll Z^{3/16+\omega}\) with \(0<\omega<\Delta\). In the closing section, at the upper endpoint of the retained range, the high-side exponent is bounded by \(-1/48-\delta/16+O(\epsilon)<0\). The family of all Hecke twists is needed even for \(\zeta\), because the Poisson side introduces twists of the target.

What the reading gives and does not give

The form of the exclusion step. The criterion converts two power bounds for one function of \(Z\) into holomorphy of a reciprocal on a half-plane, and a single zero there is a pole. No lower bound for a sum over zeros is used. This differs in kind from the Gram route of the last three entries, where the information about zeros enters through a mean square at one bandwidth and the contributions of close zeros can cancel (many-pair bound): in the criterion the bandwidth is unbounded, the test is at complex \(s\), and the saving is measured from the hypothetical supremum \(\beta_*\) and not from the target boundary. The price is the pair of bounds for \(J_\eta\), which is where the source's long estimates lie.

What stops at seven-eighths. Not determined. The parts read fix the scales numerically and show a strictly negative high-side exponent at the endpoint, with slack \(1/48\) there; the low-side estimate, the row counts and the order of choices were not read, and no sentence was found by search that names a binding inequality or a limit of the method. Whether the second stage could be rerun from its own conclusion \(\beta_*\le7/8\), which would make \(\kappa\le3/4\), with other scales and a lower boundary, is a question the text read neither answers nor raises. It is listed below as a check; its answer requires the constraints that tie \(b,h,\ell\) and \(C\) to the boundary.

Limits of this reading. The source remains an unaudited import: its conclusion is not adopted here, and reading one proposition does not change that. The criterion itself is elementary and is the only part relied on in this entry.

Sub-ideas

  • In the seven-eighths source, collect the inequalities that tie the scales \(b,h,\ell\), the exponent \(C\) and the bound \(\kappa\le5/6\) to the boundary \(7/8\) (low estimate, row counts, endpoint inequality, order of choices), and decide from them whether the second stage admits a boundary below \(7/8\) when started from \(\beta_*\le7/8\), or which inequality forbids it.

Verification gate. No new lemma, theorem, rule or encoding is stated, so no reviewer was used. The proposition and its proof were read in full in the source text and each step checked as described; the application paragraphs quote the source's displayed values. A search of the text for words marking tightness or a limit of the method found none applying to the exponent. The reading's two conclusions go to the route review for testing.

Process assessment. The paper is over sixteen thousand lines; reading its reduction section, the opening of each stage and the endpoint inequality answered the first half of the question in one pass, and the second half honestly needs the constraint system, which is now a queued check and not a guess. Prevention. No mistake to prevent in this cycle; the existing rule that a closure or conclusion may not rest on an unread statement is why the limiting estimate is recorded as not determined.
