11 October 2026 — The sign form of the Gram mechanism in the model: a solitary off-line pair moves both extreme eigenvalues by about \(\kappa\), so an eigenvalue bound at bandwidth \(\log T\) would constrain it once \(\kappa\) exceeds the on-line part's size, where the second moment needed longer bandwidths

Working proof of a small refinement in the abstract complete-grid model, flat window for the on-line bound; nothing is proved about actual zeta zeros. Extreme eigenvalue detection. Produced by Claude Code (claude-opus-5-5, low effort).

Chosen. This entry takes the route review's first lead, Weil positivity, ahead of the queue's head. It bears on off-line exclusion by asking whether a one-sided or extreme-eigenvalue bound is a weaker premise than the second moment the earlier entries used; the two tuning questions on the seven-eighths source are closed. At the goal's parameters it could give a premise at the import's own bandwidth \(\log T\), where the import's prime-side estimates live, in place of one at longer bandwidths where nothing is available. The head's sub-idea on the range of bandwidths stays needed for clusters, as the body says.

Question. The missing implication is an estimate excluding one off-line zero. In the model: how far below zero is the smallest eigenvalue of \(G_Z\) when there is an off-axis pair, how large is the largest, and what bound on either would exclude the pair, at which bandwidth? The test ends with a proved statement with its margin, or a configuration of real points that hides the pair from both extreme eigenvalues.

New mathematics. A small refinement of prop:solitary-amplification, in the model, for a solitary pair. With one off-axis pair of multiplicity \(m\) and any real points, \(G^{\mathrm{on}}\) the on-line part and \(\eta=e_y^{\mathsf T}G^{\mathrm{on}}e_y\le\lambda_{\max}(G^{\mathrm{on}})\): \(m(\kappa+1)\le\lambda_{\max}(G_Z)\le m(\kappa+1)+\lambda_{\max}(G^{\mathrm{on}})\) and \(-m(\kappa-1)\le\lambda_{\min}(G_Z)\le\min(0,-m(\kappa-1)+\eta)\). The lower bound for \(\lambda_{\max}\) was already part 4 of that proposition; the content added is the upper bound for \(\lambda_{\min}\) with its margin. On the complete grid a finite configuration with an off-axis pair always has some negative direction (route review, first lead); what is new is a direction with a quantified value, which is useful when \(m(\kappa-1)>\eta\).

Goal progress. Unchanged. The observation changes which premise the Gram mechanism needs, not whether one is available. Earlier entries evaluated the solitary bound against the second moment, by that route, and found that a constraint needs bandwidth \(\theta\log T\) with \(\theta>1\); they were scoped to the second moment and this entry looks at the other norms. The Hilbert–Schmidt norm is at least \(\sqrt N\), because a trace \(N\) is spread over a rank at most \(N\), so the pair shows in it only if \(\kappa>\sqrt N\). The operator norm has no such floor: in the model a bound \(\lambda_{\max}(G)\le B\) at bandwidth \(\Lambda\) gives \(\delta\le(\log B+\log(4\delta\Lambda))/\Lambda\) for a solitary pair with \(\delta\Lambda\ge1\), and with \(B=T^{\varepsilon}\) at \(\Lambda=\log T\) this is \(\delta\le\varepsilon+o(1)\). When all points are real the form's largest eigenvalue is bounded by local counts, so this premise follows from the goal; and a bound of that size for the corresponding sums over primes is classically of the strength of a zero-free strip near height \(T\), so the premise is a local restatement of the goal and not only a consequence of it (the reviewer's remark; not derived here). The import supplies the trace and the second moment at that bandwidth, which give no bound for an extreme eigenvalue below order \(\sqrt N\). Bearing. At the goal's parameters this gives nothing: it names a premise at the import's own bandwidth that would constrain a solitary off-line zero, and the premise is not supplied. The cycle was spent on it because the review queued the sign form as the one open item of the Gram mechanism that could change what the route needs.

General statement. prop:extreme-eigenvalue-detection, for every bandwidth, every admissible window and every configuration with exactly one off-axis pair, on the complete grid; the bound for \(\lambda_{\max}(G^{\mathrm{on}})\) is lem:real-configuration-bound, flat window.

Statement and proof

Setting as in the pair atom spectrum entry: complete grid (all \(k\in\mathbb Z\)), real even window, \(G_Z=G^{\mathrm{on}}+B\), where \(G^{\mathrm{on}}\succeq0\) is the sum of the on-line atoms and, for one off-axis pair \(z,\bar z\) of multiplicity \(m\) at distance \(\delta\), \(B=m[(\kappa+1)e_xe_x^{\mathsf T}-(\kappa-1)e_ye_y^{\mathsf T}]\) with orthonormal real \(e_x,e_y\) and \(\kappa=\kappa(\delta)>1\). Write \(\lambda_{\min}\) for the infimum of the quadratic form on unit vectors of \(\ell^2(\mathbb Z)\); as \(G_Z\) has finite rank this infimum is at most \(0\).

Proposition (extreme eigenvalue detection). prop:extreme-eigenvalue-detection. For such a configuration, with \(\eta=e_y^{\mathsf T}G^{\mathrm{on}}e_y\in[0,\lambda_{\max}(G^{\mathrm{on}})]\), \[m(\kappa+1)\ \le\ \lambda_{\max}(G_Z)\ \le\ m(\kappa+1)+\lambda_{\max}(G^{\mathrm{on}}),\qquad -m(\kappa-1)\ \le\ \lambda_{\min}(G_Z)\ \le\ \min\bigl(0,\,-m(\kappa-1)+\eta\bigr).\] In particular \(\|G_Z\|_{\mathrm{op}}\ge m(\kappa+1)\), and when \(m(\kappa-1)>\eta\) the form takes the value \(-m(\kappa-1)+\eta<0\) at \(e_y\). For the flat window, if the real points have at most \(M_{\mathrm{loc}}\) per cell of length \(2\pi/\Lambda\) over \(K\) consecutive cells, then \(\eta\le\lambda_{\max}(G^{\mathrm{on}})\le M_{\mathrm{loc}}(3+\tfrac2\pi(1+\ln K))\).

Proof. By \(e_x\cdot e_y=0\), \(e_x^{\mathsf T}Be_x=m(\kappa+1)\) and \(e_y^{\mathsf T}Be_y=-m(\kappa-1)\). Since \(G^{\mathrm{on}}\succeq0\), \(e_x^{\mathsf T}G_Ze_x\ge m(\kappa+1)\): the first inequality. Weyl's inequality for bounded symmetric operators gives \(\lambda_{\max}(G_Z)\le\lambda_{\max}(G^{\mathrm{on}})+\lambda_{\max}(B)\). For every unit \(u\), \(u^{\mathsf T}G_Zu\ge u^{\mathsf T}Bu\ge-m(\kappa-1)\). And \(e_y^{\mathsf T}G_Ze_y=-m(\kappa-1)+\eta\). The last sentence is lem:real-configuration-bound applied to the real points. \(\square\)

The two sides are not symmetric. Detection through \(\lambda_{\max}\) needs nothing about the real points: \(\|G_Z\|_{\mathrm{op}}\ge m(\kappa+1)\) always. Detection through \(\lambda_{\min}\) needs \(\eta\) to be smaller than \(m(\kappa-1)\), that is a bound on the real points near the pair.

What the real points can do. There is no dimension-count threshold at which real points outnumbering grid points hide the pair on the complete grid: the test at \(e_y\) loses only \(\eta\), which is at most \(\lambda_{\max}(G^{\mathrm{on}})\) and so at most a constant times the number of real points per cell, with a logarithm of the number of cells. The density of real points relative to the grid does matter, linearly, through that number. The question of the proposed next step about room in the grid concerns the import's cut grid, which this proposition does not treat: the transfer of the \(e_y\) test to a cut grid, by a tail bound as in part 5 of solitary amplification, is not done here.

Thresholds (a computation, not a theorem about \(\zeta\)). For the flat window \(\kappa=\sinh b/b\ge e^{b}/(4b)\) for \(b=\delta\Lambda\ge1\). If \(\|G_Z\|_{\mathrm{op}}\le B\) for a configuration with a solitary pair, then \(e^{\delta\Lambda}\le4\delta\Lambda B\), so \(\delta\le(\log B+\log(4\delta\Lambda))/\Lambda\). From a one-sided bound \(\lambda_{\min}(G_Z)\ge-B\) the same follows with \(B\) replaced by \(B+1+\eta\), hence by \(B+1+\lambda_{\max}(G^{\mathrm{on}})\), for \(m=1\). Compare the second moment: \(\|G_Z\|_{\mathrm{HS}}^2\ge\operatorname{tr}(G_Z)^2/\operatorname{rank}(G_Z)\ge N\) for \(N\) points, so a second-moment bound sees the pair only when \(\kappa^2\) exceeds the number of points, which for a configuration of about \((T/2\pi)\log T\) points means \(\delta\Lambda>\tfrac12\log T\): at \(\Lambda=\log T\) never, since \(\delta<1/2\). The extreme eigenvalues see a solitary pair as soon as \(\kappa\) exceeds \(\lambda_{\max}(G^{\mathrm{on}})\). In the flat-window complete-grid model, if all other points are real with at most \(O(\log T)\) points in any unit interval, then each cell of length \(2\pi/\log T\) holds \(O(\log T)\) points and \(\ln K=O(\log T)\), so \(\lambda_{\max}(G^{\mathrm{on}})=O(\log^2T)\) and at \(\Lambda=\log T\) every \(\delta\ge C\log\log T/\log T\) is seen. The import's window is smoothed and its grid cut, and the on-line bound is proved for the flat window only; nothing is claimed for the import's form.

What the premise would be. An upper bound for \(\lambda_{\max}\), or a lower bound for \(\lambda_{\min}\), of the import's Gram form at bandwidth \(\log T\), of size \(T^{\varepsilon}\). Since \(c^{\mathsf T}Gc\) is \(N_\varphi^{-1}\sum_\rho m_\rho H(z_\rho)^2\) with \(H\) the transform of a function supported in \([-\Lambda/2,\Lambda/2]\), and \(H^2\) is the transform of a function supported in \([-\Lambda,\Lambda]\), the explicit formula for a full or smoothly weighted zero sum makes the form linear in the prime powers up to \(e^{\Lambda}\), not bilinear; I state this as the expected form of the prime side, not derived here and not relied on. It does not apply as stated to a sharply cut finite configuration. The import's method does not address such a bound: the Montgomery–Vaughan inequality bounds the bilinear off-diagonal sum that appears in the second moment.

Several pairs. With \(p\) pairs the test at \(e_{y_1}\) gains positive terms \((\kappa_j+1)(e_{x_j}\cdot e_{y_1})^2\) from the other pairs, so the solitary argument does not carry over, as for the norm. Combining earlier statements: \(\|G_Z\|_{\mathrm{op}}\ge\|B\|_{\mathrm{op}}-\lambda_{\max}(G^{\mathrm{on}})\ge\|B\|_{\mathrm{HS}}/\sqrt{2n}-\lambda_{\max}(G^{\mathrm{on}})\), since \(B\) has rank at most \(2n\), and thm:resonance-free-bandwidth bounds \(\|B\|_{\mathrm{HS}}\) below at some bandwidth in a range. So an operator-norm bound over that range bounds the largest \(\delta\) with a loss \(\sqrt{2n}\), where \(n\) is not bounded and the loss can be a power of \(T\); the range's length is the head item's open sub-idea and is still needed for clusters.

Numerical control

Nonvacuity. The script eigen_detection.py computes, for the flat window on a grid cut at \(|k|\le3\cdot10^4\), the extreme eigenvalues of the nonzero spectrum of \(G_Z\) for one off-axis pair among random real points, for \(\Lambda\in\{16,24\}\), \(\delta\in\{0.1,0.25,0.45\}\) and local ratios of grid density to point density \(0.5,1,2\) (31 to 183 real points within height 12 of the pair), eighteen configurations. The grid has 60001 indices against at most 185 points, so this tests the effect of local density only, not any dimension count. In all eighteen, \(\lambda_{\max}-(\kappa+1)\) lies between \(0.12\) and \(3.1\) and \(\lambda_{\min}+(\kappa-1)\) between \(0.11\) and \(2.3\), both within \([0,\lambda_{\max}(G^{\mathrm{on}})]\), with \(\lambda_{\max}(G^{\mathrm{on}})\) between \(2.1\) and \(6.4\) and \(\kappa\) from \(1.5\) to \(2269\) (output). The pass tolerance is \(2\cdot10^{-4}(\kappa+1)+10^{-3}\), below \(\lambda_{\max}(G^{\mathrm{on}})\) in every row; the first version used \(5\cdot10^{-3}(\kappa+1)\), which at \(\kappa=2269\) exceeded the interval's width and made the pass flag empty there. In seven rows \(\kappa-1<\lambda_{\max}(G^{\mathrm{on}})\), so the proposition's bound through \(\lambda_{\max}(G^{\mathrm{on}})\) says nothing; the smallest eigenvalue is negative there all the same (for instance between \(-0.02\) and \(-1.04\) in the six rows with \(\delta=0.1\)), in line with the automatic negative direction on the complete grid and with \(\eta\) being smaller than \(\lambda_{\max}(G^{\mathrm{on}})\). A wrong sign in either one-sided bound would put an eigenvalue outside its interval by an amount of order one. The control is not registered as a claim.

Sub-ideas

  • Derive the prime side of the quadratic form \(c^{\mathsf T}Gc\) for the import's construction at bandwidth \(\log T\) from its explicit formula, checking which zero set and weight its form uses; state which sum over prime powers bounds \(\lambda_{\max}\) and \(\lambda_{\min}\). If the premise is equivalent to a zero-free strip near height \(T\), the lead closes as a restatement of the open problem; if the form's structure gives any unconditional bound beyond the second moment's, that bound is the result.

Follow-up. Continuing: the lead asked whether the sign of the form, instead of its norm, detects an off-line zero and with what margin. In the model, for a solitary pair, the form's value at \(e_y\) is \(-m(\kappa-1)+\eta\), negative with margin when \(m(\kappa-1)>\eta\); for several pairs it inherits the cluster question. The lead still bears on off-line exclusion through the prime side of the extreme eigenvalues, which is the sub-idea above, with its closing outcome stated there, and the next attempt. Record search. For that attempt the registry was searched for extreme eigenvalue operator norm detects off-axis pair, Weil positivity compression, and explicit formula primes: it returns this notebook's own claims (prop:many-pair-norm-bound, lem:pair-atom-spectrum) and the imported density theorem, and no record of a prime-side expression for the form.

Reviewer's suggestions, unverified

  • An exact form of the loss at \(e_y\): \(\eta=(2/(\kappa-1))\sum_\gamma m_\gamma(\Im g(z-\gamma))^2\), which for the flat window and real points of density \(D\) per unit height is about \(8\pi D/\Lambda\), four times the number of points per cell, with no logarithm and only points within height about \(\delta\) of the pair counting. Record. Search for extreme eigenvalue operator norm detects off-axis pair returned this notebook's claims only; this would sharpen prop:extreme-eigenvalue-detection.
  • A Plancherel–Pólya bound in place of the row-sum bound would give \(\lambda_{\max}(G^{\mathrm{on}})\le CM_{\mathrm{loc}}\|\varphi\|_\infty^2\Lambda/\|\varphi\|_2^2\) for bounded windows, without the logarithm and covering the import's window. Record. Search for Paley-Wiener bandlimited returned no record on these objects; this would generalize lem:real-configuration-bound.
  • Higher moments give nothing beyond the second: a \(k\)-th moment in its diagonal range needs \(\Lambda\lesssim(2/k)\log T\), where \(\kappa^k\le T\), so every \(k\) returns \(\delta\le1/2+o(1)\). Record. Search for second moment Hilbert-Schmidt trace rank lower bound returned the imported density theorem only.
  • A caution, from the reviewer's memory and unread: positivity of Weil's form is known unconditionally for test functions of small support; if so, for the full zero set an off-line zero need not give a negative direction at small bandwidth, and the finite-configuration statement does not transfer. Record. Search for Weil positivity compression returned no record; the sub-idea now asks which zero set and weight the import's form uses.

Suggestion check. The first suggestion's formula is verified: \(G^{\mathrm{on}}=N_\varphi^{-1}\sum_\gamma m_\gamma v_\gamma v_\gamma^{\mathsf T}\) and \(e_y=y/|y|\) with \(|y|^2=(\kappa-1)N_\varphi/2\), so \(\eta=\sum_\gamma m_\gamma(v_\gamma\cdot y)^2/(N_\varphi|y|^2)\); and \(v_\gamma\cdot y=\Im(v_\gamma^{\mathsf T}v_z)=N_\varphi\Im g(\gamma-z)\) because \(v_\gamma\) is real, which gives \(\eta=(2/(\kappa-1))\sum_\gamma m_\gamma(\Im g(z-\gamma))^2\), \(g\) being even. Its evaluation as \(8\pi D/\Lambda\) is not verified. The other suggestions are not verified and nothing rests on them.

Verification gate. Dependencies re-read: the pair atom spectrum, solitary amplification and the real configuration bound, from their full records. The constant in the threshold was \(2\) in one place and \(4\) in another; \(4\) is right, for \(\delta\Lambda\ge1\). The bound for \(\lambda_{\min}\) is now stated as a minimum with \(0\) and with \(\eta\) in place of \(\lambda_{\max}(G^{\mathrm{on}})\). 11 October 2026 — The prime side of the Gram form as a weighted square integral: an elementary lemma from the density source's definitions, which as proved gives only the trivial bound; the sign lead stays open on a localization

Working proof of an elementary lemma from the density source's definitions, read in its Lean file; it yields the trivial bound only, and nothing is proved about zeta zeros. Prime-side form. Produced by Claude Code (claude-opus-5-5, low effort).

Chosen. This entry continues the route review's first lead ahead of the queue's head, as the previous entry's next step named: it bears on off-line exclusion through the prime side of the extreme eigenvalues, and the lead's outcome (closure as a restatement, or an unconditional bound) depended on this reading, while the head's remaining sub-idea concerns the range of bandwidths for clusters and does not.

Question. The missing implication for off-line exclusion is an estimate excluding one off-line zero. The previous entry found that, in the model, a bound for an extreme eigenvalue of the Gram form at bandwidth \(\log T\) would constrain a solitary off-line pair, and left open what that bound is on the prime side. This cycle continues the review's first lead: read the import's prime-side expression for the form, state which sum over prime powers bounds the extreme eigenvalues, and decide whether the premise is a restatement of the goal. At the goal's parameters it could give an unconditional bound for an extreme eigenvalue beyond what the second moment gives, if the form's structure allowed one. The test ends with the expression read from the source and the premise stated in primes, or the finding that the source's formula does not have that form.

New mathematics. An elementary lemma for the source's construction. For real \(c\), a form \(\sum c_kc_l\int\widehat\varphi(\tau-\tau_k)\widehat\varphi(\tau-\tau_l)\nu(\tau)d\tau\) with bounded \(\nu\) equals \(\int H_c^2\nu\), with \(\int H_c^2\le2\pi L\|\varphi\|_\infty^2|c|^2\), so it lies between \(\inf\nu\) and \(\sup\nu\) times that integral; and for \(\nu=P_X\), the truncated prime sum, each diagonal entry is \(-2\sum_n\Lambda(n)n^{-1/2}(\varphi*\varphi)(\log n)\cos(\tau_k\log n)\). The first part gives no information beyond the trivial bound, because the supremum of \(|P_X|\) over the whole line is attained at \(\tau=0\); the second gives one proved implication from an eigenvalue bound to a bound for a smoothed prime sum at the grid heights.

Goal progress. Unchanged. What is proved: the prime-sum part \(G^{P}\) of the import's form has operator norm at most \(2\pi L\|\varphi\|_\infty^2\cdot\tfrac1\pi\sum_{n\le X}\Lambda(n)n^{-1/2}\), the trivial bound, of order \(\sqrt X\), which corresponds to \(\delta\le1/2\) for a solitary pair in the model; and any bound for the extreme eigenvalues of \(G^{P}\) on a set of grid indices bounds the smoothed prime sums at those grid heights. What is not proved: that a bound for \(P_X(\tau)\) only for \(\tau\) near \([T,2T]\) bounds the eigenvalues, which needs a localization; and whether the eigenvalue premise is equivalent to the goal in local form. I expected to close the lead as a restatement and the evidence does not support closing: the closure would rest on an unproved localization and an unproved converse. Bearing. At the goal's parameters this gives nothing: the only bound obtained is the trivial one. The cycle was spent on it because the lead's outcome depended on reading the prime side.

General statement. lem:prime-side-form-bound, for every \(L>0\), every bounded real even window supported in \([-L/2,L/2]\), every bounded measurable density and every finitely supported real coefficient vector on a grid of step \(2\pi/L\).

What the source defines

Read in Defs.lean of the retained density project. With \(L=\lambda l\), \(X=e^{L}\) (the definition of \(l\) was read in an earlier cycle as \(\log(T/2\pi)\)): the window is \(\varphi(u)=\varrho((L/2-|u|)/w)\) for a taper profile \(\varrho\) (monotone, \(0\) on \((-\infty,0]\), \(1\) on \([1,\infty)\)), so \(\varphi\) is real, even, \(0\le\varphi\le1\), with support contained in \([-L/2,L/2]\) (the source's docstring says exactly that interval); the grid is \(\tau_k=T+2\pi k/L\), \(k\in\mathbb Z\); \(a=L^{-1}\int\varphi^2\). The densities are \(\mu(\tau)=\tfrac1{2\pi}\Re\tfrac{\Gamma'}{\Gamma}(\tfrac14+\tfrac{i\tau}2)-\tfrac{\log\pi}{2\pi}\), \(\Pi_X(\tau)=\tfrac1{2\pi(1/4+\tau^2)}+\tfrac1\pi\Re\tfrac{X^{s}-1}{s}\) at \(s=\tfrac12+i\tau\), \(P_X(\tau)=-\tfrac1\pi\sum_{n\le X}\Lambda(n)n^{-1/2}\cos(\tau\log n)\), and \(\nu_X=\mu+\Pi_X+P_X\). The prime-side entry is \(G_{kl}=\int_{\mathbb R}\widehat\varphi(\tau-\tau_k)\widehat\varphi(\tau-\tau_l)\nu_X(\tau)\,d\tau\), with \(\widehat\varphi\) real on the real line, and the matrix used is \((G_{kl})_{0\le k,l<d}\), \(d=\lfloor LT/2\pi\rfloor\); hat units divide by \(aL^2\). The zero-side entry is a sum of \(m_\rho\widehat\varphi(\gamma_\rho-\tau_k)\widehat\varphi(\gamma_\rho-\tau_l)\) over the carrier of an abstract zero configuration, with \(\gamma_\rho=(\rho-1/2)/i\); docstrings say the sum runs over the distinct nontrivial zeros of either sign of ordinate and that the source's explicit-formula hypothesis gives equality of the two matrices. The source's transform is its own function, whose convention I did not read; by a docstring the test function \(\varphi(u)e^{-i\tau_ku}\) has transform \(\widehat\varphi(z-\tau_k)\), which indicates the kernel \(e^{+iuz}\) without a factor \(1/2\pi\). For an even window this differs from the convention \(\int f(u)e^{-iuz}du\) used below by nothing, and the hat normalization \(aL^2=L\|\varphi\|_2^2\) is consistent with no \(1/2\pi\).

The lemma

Lemma (prime-side form bound). lem:prime-side-form-bound. Let \(L>0\), \(\tau_k=\tau_0+2\pi k/L\) for \(k\in\mathbb Z\), and let \(\varphi\) be real, even, bounded and measurable with support in \([-L/2,L/2]\), \(\widehat\varphi(z)=\int\varphi(u)e^{-iuz}du\). For a finitely supported real \(c=(c_k)\) put \(H_c(\tau)=\sum_kc_k\widehat\varphi(\tau-\tau_k)\). Then \(H_c\) is real on \(\mathbb R\), \[\int_{\mathbb R}H_c(\tau)^2\,d\tau=2\pi\int_{-L/2}^{L/2}\varphi(u)^2\Bigl|\sum_kc_ke^{iu\tau_k}\Bigr|^2du\ \le\ 2\pi L\,\|\varphi\|_\infty^2\,|c|^2,\] and for every bounded measurable \(\nu:\mathbb R\to\mathbb R\) the form \(Q_\nu(c)=\sum_{k,l}c_kc_l\int\widehat\varphi(\tau-\tau_k)\widehat\varphi(\tau-\tau_l)\nu(\tau)\,d\tau\) satisfies \[Q_\nu(c)=\int_{\mathbb R}H_c(\tau)^2\,\nu(\tau)\,d\tau,\qquad\inf\nu\cdot\!\int H_c^2\ \le\ Q_\nu(c)\ \le\ \sup\nu\cdot\!\int H_c^2.\] Consequently the matrix of \(Q_\nu\) on any set of grid indices has largest eigenvalue at most \(2\pi L\|\varphi\|_\infty^2\max(\sup\nu,0)\) and smallest at least \(2\pi L\|\varphi\|_\infty^2\min(\inf\nu,0)\). For \(\nu(\tau)=\cos(\tau v)\) with real \(v\), the diagonal entry is \(\int\widehat\varphi(\tau-\tau_k)^2\cos(\tau v)\,d\tau=2\pi(\varphi*\varphi)(v)\cos(\tau_kv)\); hence for \(\nu=P_X\) with \(X\ge e^{L}\), \[G^{P}_{kk}=-2\sum_{n}\Lambda(n)\,n^{-1/2}\,(\varphi*\varphi)(\log n)\,\cos(\tau_k\log n),\] the sum being over \(n\le e^{L}\) automatically.

Proof. \(\widehat\varphi(\tau-\tau_k)=\int\varphi(u)e^{iu\tau_k}e^{-iu\tau}du\), so \(H_c\) is the transform of \(\varphi C_c\) with \(C_c(u)=\sum_kc_ke^{iu\tau_k}\); it is real on the real line because \(\widehat\varphi\) is (\(\varphi\) real and even) and \(c\) is real. As \(\varphi C_c\) is bounded with compact support it is in \(L^2\), and Plancherel for this transform gives \(\int|H_c|^2=2\pi\int\varphi^2|C_c|^2\). The function \(|C_c(u)|^2=|\sum_kc_ke^{2\pi iku/L}|^2\) does not depend on \(\tau_0\), has period \(L\), and its integral over any period is \(L\sum_kc_k^2\) by orthogonality; the support hypothesis restricts the integral to one period, and \(\varphi^2\le\|\varphi\|_\infty^2\) gives the inequality. Since \(H_c\in L^2\) and \(\nu\) is bounded, \(H_c^2\nu\) is integrable and expanding the square gives \(Q_\nu(c)=\int H_c^2\nu\); the two-sided bound follows from \(H_c^2\ge0\), and the eigenvalue bounds from it and the bound on \(\int H_c^2\), a principal submatrix having its extreme eigenvalues between those of the whole form. For the diagonal entry, \(\widehat\varphi^2\) is the transform of \(\varphi*\varphi\), which is even, continuous and supported in \([-L,L]\), so by Fourier inversion \(\int\widehat\varphi(t)^2e^{itv}dt=2\pi(\varphi*\varphi)(v)\); with \(t=\tau-\tau_k\) and the real part, \(\int\widehat\varphi(\tau-\tau_k)^2\cos(\tau v)d\tau=2\pi(\varphi*\varphi)(v)\cos(\tau_kv)\). Summing over \(n\) with the coefficients of \(P_X\) gives the last formula, and terms with \(\log n>L\) vanish. \(\square\)

The lemma applies to \(P_X\) and to \(\Pi_X\), which is bounded (\(|X^{s}|=\sqrt X\), \(|s|\ge1/2\)), not to \(\mu\), which grows logarithmically; the source's integrals with \(\mu\) converge by the decay of \(\widehat\varphi\), which is not examined here.

What the lemma gives, and what it does not

Only the trivial bound. \(P_X\) is a cosine sum with coefficients of one sign, so \(\sup_\tau|P_X(\tau)|=|P_X(0)|=\tfrac1\pi\sum_{n\le X}\Lambda(n)n^{-1/2}\) and \(\inf P_X=P_X(0)\). The lemma's bound with the supremum over the whole line is therefore the trivial one: in hat units \(\|\widehat{G^{P}}\|_{\mathrm{op}}\le\tfrac{2}{aL}\sum_{n\le X}\Lambda(n)n^{-1/2}\), of order \(\sqrt X/L\), and similarly for the \(\Pi_X\) part. If the chain below were repaired and the \(\mu\) part bounded, this would correspond to \(\delta\le1/2+O(\log L/L)\) for a solitary pair, no information, at every \(\lambda\). I did not ask this before drafting; the reviewer pointed it out.

One proved implication. For any set of grid indices containing \(k\), \(\lambda_{\min}\le G^{P}_{kk}\le\lambda_{\max}\) for the matrix of \(G^{P}\) on that set. So a bound for the extreme eigenvalues of \(G^{P}\) implies the same bound for \(2|\sum_n\Lambda(n)n^{-1/2}(\varphi*\varphi)(\log n)\cos(\tau_k\log n)|\) at the grid heights: a prime sum of length \(e^{L}\) with the smooth weight \(\varphi*\varphi\) (the triangle \(L-|v|\) for the flat window), at heights in \([T,2T]\). This direction needs no localization. It concerns \(G^{P}\); the premise of the previous entry is a bound for the whole form, and passing between the two needs the diagonal of the \(\mu+\Pi_X\) part, which is not computed here and belongs to the first sub-idea.

What would be needed for the other direction. To bound the eigenvalues by the size of \(P_X\) only for \(\tau\) near \([T,2T]\), one needs that \(\int H_c^2\) outside a neighbourhood of that interval is small for \(c\) supported on \(0\le k<d\), by the decay of \(\widehat\varphi\); the reviewer sketches it with \(|\widehat\varphi(r)|\le C/(wr^2)\) and a neighbourhood of width \(T^{1/4+\epsilon}\). It is not proved here, and without it no statement of the form "a bound \(T^{\varepsilon}\) for the prime sum near height \(T\) bounds the eigenvalues" is available. The same localization is needed for the \(\mu\) part.

A conditional chain, with its defects. I drafted a chain from the source's explicit formula, a single off-line pair and the tail hypothesis of part 5 of prop:solitary-amplification to an inequality between \(\kappa\) and the trivial bound. As drafted its hypothesis cannot hold: the source's zero set has both signs of ordinate, so an off-line zero comes with its mirror image and there are at least two conjugate pairs in the variable \(z\); part 5 is stated for one pair and a finite configuration. The mirror pair is at distance at least \(2T\) and should be negligible by decay, which is not checked. Since the chain's conclusion is the trivial bound in any case, it is not repaired here and is listed below.

Sub-ideas

11 October 2026 — The Hermite–Biehler lead for finite configurations: the half-plane sign of the logarithmic derivative is the zero-free statement itself, and the local phase-monotonicity condition on the real line can fail for a simple off-line pair only when it is closer to the line than half the gap between the on-line points around it

Working proofs of two elementary statements about finite configurations; the lead's sources are not read and it stays open. Sign criteria. Produced by Claude Code (claude-opus-5-5, low effort).

Chosen. This entry takes the route review's second lead ahead of the queue's head: the first lead has had two consecutive entries declaring no bearing (the queue tool's count at this cycle's start) and the rule asks for a different item, and this one bears on off-line exclusion by proposing a statement of a different kind, a sign or monotonicity condition for one function, where the Gram mechanism's forms all needed a bound on prime sums. At the goal's parameters it could give a criterion in which a single off-line zero cannot be cancelled by others and which some available estimate controls; if it only restates the zero-free statement, it gives nothing.

Question. The missing implication is an estimate excluding one off-line zero. For a finite configuration with polynomial \(p\), what does the Hermite–Biehler criterion for \(p+ip'\) say in terms of the points, in its half-plane form and on the real line, and does any quantity the imports control bound the relevant function? The test ends with such a bound, or with the identity showing that the criterion is the same sum over the points in another guise.

New mathematics. Two elementary statements for a finite multiset of points with \(\Phi(s)=\sum_\rho m_\rho/(s-\rho)\). (a) All points have \(\Re\rho\le\sigma_0\) exactly when \(\Re\Phi(s)>0\) for all \(\Re s>\sigma_0\). (b) In the variable \(z\), with real points \(\Gamma\) and one conjugate pair \(\gamma\pm i\delta\) of multiplicity \(m\), the function \(\mathcal L(x)=\sum_wm_w/(x-w)^2\) on the real line (the Laguerre expression \((p'^2-pp'')/p^2\), whose sign is opposite to that of the phase derivative of \(p+ip'\)) is negative at \(x=\gamma\) exactly when \(\sum_\Gamma m'/(\gamma-\gamma')^2<2m/\delta^2\), and is nonnegative everywhere when the real points have gaps of length at most \(2\delta/\sqrt m\) covering \((\gamma-\delta,\gamma+\delta)\). Both are elementary; part (a) is the finite model of an equivalence I believe is in the literature for \(\xi'/\xi\) (the reviewer names sources from memory, unread), and no novelty is claimed.

Goal progress. Unchanged. In half-plane form the criterion is equivalent, for finite configurations and at every boundary, to the zero-free statement it is meant to prove: it is not a weaker premise, and a pole cannot be cancelled, as in the continuation criterion. On the real line the criterion has a local part, monotonicity of the phase, and a global part, the count of real zeros. The local part does not fail for an off-line pair whose distance from the line is at least \(\sqrt m/2\) times the gaps between the on-line points around it. The global part detects every pair, and is the statement that all points are real. The Gram form's extreme eigenvalues are proved to see a solitary pair at distance above about \(\log\log T/\log T\); between the two scales neither statement gives detection, which is a gap in what is proved and not a complementarity. No estimate for either function to the left of \(\Re s=1\) is supplied by the imports as read. The lead's own sources (the Hermite–Biehler theorem in general form, structure functions of de Branges type) were not read, so no conclusion about them is drawn. Bearing. At the goal's parameters this gives nothing: the half-plane form and the count are the goal's statement, and the local condition does not fail for pairs farther from the line than the local gap. The cycle was spent on it because it was the one queued item proposing a different kind of statement.

General statement. prop:log-derivative-sign-criteria, for every nonempty finite multiset of complex points (part a) and every finite conjugation-closed configuration with exactly one off-axis pair (part b).

Review. The stand-in review after the tenth entry raised five objections. Its test has three constructed refused cases and three accepted ones; run over every notebook's research entries (2137, by a scratch count reported in that commit's message) it refuses one, the entry that prompted it, so it is a keyword check that catches the wording met and is untested on other wordings of the same class. (3) Prevention paragraphs named no framework change made in the turn: accepted for the turns it quotes; the finisher check and a correction to the test runner's CPU line are committed since. The two build items that asked for them stay queued, because an entry develops one top-level item and this one develops a lead; an entry of their own will record them. (4) The withdrawal of the \(5/6\) floor was reported without a Prevention paragraph in that reply: accepted, and not fixed; the reply described a correction without the words the Stop hook looks for, which is how it passed, and I have not changed the hook. Queued as a sub-idea below. (5) The suite's 16 s run should have been investigated before committing: accepted; the whole suite, run alone since, took 7.7 s for 866 tests and passed (the launcher's log of this cycle), and the runner's line that attributed the shared computation slice's time to the run itself is corrected. That the 16 s run was caused by other sessions' jobs is an inference from these two facts, not a measurement of that run.

Statements and proofs

Proposition (logarithmic derivative sign criteria). prop:log-derivative-sign-criteria.

(a) Half-plane form. Let \(R\) be a nonempty finite set of complex numbers with multiplicities \(m_\rho\ge1\), \(\Phi(s)=\sum_{\rho\in R}m_\rho/(s-\rho)\), and \(\sigma_0\) real. Then every \(\rho\in R\) has \(\Re\rho\le\sigma_0\) if and only if \(\Re\Phi(s)>0\) for every \(s\) with \(\Re s>\sigma_0\).

(b) Real-line form. Let \(\Gamma\) be a finite set of real points with multiplicities \(m_{\gamma'}\ge1\), and \(w_\pm=\gamma\pm i\delta\), \(\delta>0\), a conjugate pair of multiplicity \(m\). For real \(x\notin\Gamma\) put \[\mathcal L(x)=\sum_{\gamma'\in\Gamma}{m_{\gamma'}\over(x-\gamma')^2}+{2m\,((x-\gamma)^2-\delta^2)\over((x-\gamma)^2+\delta^2)^2},\] which equals \(\sum_wm_w/(x-w)^2\) over all points and \((p'^2-pp'')/p^2\) for \(p(x)=\prod_w(x-w)^{m_w}\). Then: (i) if \(\gamma\notin\Gamma\), \(\mathcal L(\gamma)<0\) if and only if \(\sum_\Gamma m_{\gamma'}/(\gamma-\gamma')^2<2m/\delta^2\); (ii) the pair's term is at least \(-2m/\delta^2\) and is negative only for \(|x-\gamma|<\delta\); (iii) if every \(x\in(\gamma-\delta,\gamma+\delta)\setminus\Gamma\) lies between two consecutive points \(a<b\) of \(\Gamma\) with \(b-a\le2\delta/\sqrt m\), then \(\mathcal L(x)\ge0\) for all real \(x\notin\Gamma\).

Proof. (a) \(\Re\,1/(s-\rho)=\Re(s-\rho)/|s-\rho|^2\), which is positive when \(\Re s>\Re\rho\); if all \(\Re\rho\le\sigma_0\) every term is positive on \(\Re s>\sigma_0\), and \(R\) is nonempty. Conversely let \(\Re\rho_0>\sigma_0\) and \(s=\rho_0-\epsilon\) with \(0<\epsilon<\Re\rho_0-\sigma_0\), so \(\Re s>\sigma_0\); then \(\Phi(s)=-m_{\rho_0}/\epsilon+\sum_{\rho\ne\rho_0}m_\rho/(s-\rho)\), and the sum stays bounded as \(\epsilon\to0\), so \(\Re\Phi(s)<0\) for small \(\epsilon\). (b) For the pair, \(m/(x-w_+)^2+m/(x-w_-)^2=2m\Re(x-\gamma+i\delta)^{-2}=2m((x-\gamma)^2-\delta^2)/((x-\gamma)^2+\delta^2)^2\); the identities with \(p\) are \((p'/p)'=(pp''-p'^2)/p^2\) and \(p'/p=\sum_wm_w/(x-w)\). (i) At \(x=\gamma\) the pair's term is \(-2m/\delta^2\). (ii) With \(t=(x-\gamma)^2\ge0\), \((t-\delta^2)/(t+\delta^2)^2\ge-1/\delta^2\) because \((t-\delta^2)\delta^2+(t+\delta^2)^2=t^2+3t\delta^2\ge0\), and the term's sign is that of \(t-\delta^2\). (iii) For \(|x-\gamma|\ge\delta\) all terms are nonnegative. For \(x\in(\gamma-\delta,\gamma+\delta)\setminus\Gamma\) between consecutive \(a<b\), \(1/(x-a)^2+1/(x-b)^2\ge8/(b-a)^2\), the minimum being at the midpoint by convexity and symmetry, so the real points contribute at least \(8/(b-a)^2\ge2m/\delta^2\), which by (ii) outweighs the pair. \(\square\)

Degenerate cases. In (a) the empty set is excluded: then \(\Phi\equiv0\) and the right side fails while the left holds vacuously. Points with \(\Re\rho=\sigma_0\) are allowed and the equivalence holds with them. No symmetry of \(R\) is needed in (a) itself; its translation to real points below uses that the configuration is closed under conjugation. In (iii) equality occurs (two simple points at \(\gamma\pm\delta/\sqrt m\), \(x=\gamma\)). In (b), with \(\Gamma\) empty (i) says \(\mathcal L(\gamma)=-2m/\delta^2<0\) always and the hypothesis of (iii) cannot hold; a pair whose abscissa is a real point (\(\gamma\in\Gamma\)) is outside (i), and (ii), (iii) remain valid on the complement of \(\Gamma\); with more than one off-axis pair the statement does not apply, the pairs' terms adding.

What the two forms say

The half-plane form. With \(s=1/2+iz\) the function \(\Phi\) is the configuration's part of a logarithmic derivative, and for a polynomial \(p\) in the variable \(z\) the zeros of \(p+ip'\), apart from multiple roots of \(p\) (which are zeros of both), are the points where \(p'/p=i\). With \(\Re s>1/2\) corresponding to \(\Im z<0\) and \(\Re\Phi=\Im(p'/p)\), part (a) says: all points have \(\Im w\ge0\) exactly when \(\Im(p'/p)>0\) on the lower half-plane. For a configuration closed under conjugation (\(p\) real) this is: all points are real exactly when \(\Im(p'/p)<0\) throughout the upper half-plane, and then \(p'/p\) cannot equal \(i\) there, nor at a real point that is not a root, where \(p'/p\) is real; so \(p+ip'\) has no zeros in the closed upper half-plane except multiple roots of \(p\). This is one direction of the Hermite–Biehler criterion, obtained here from the sign. So in the model the criterion, in the form "the logarithmic derivative has positive real part to the right of a line", is the same statement as "no point lies to the right of that line", at every line. It has the continuation criterion's virtue, a single point to the right makes the function negative near it whatever the other points do, and its cost: a proof of the sign condition on a half-plane is a proof of the zero-free half-plane. For \(\xi\) the corresponding sum \(\Re\sum_\rho1/(s-\rho)=\sum_\rho(\sigma-\beta)/|s-\rho|^2\) is positive term by term to the right of all zeros, so the sign is known on \(\Re s\ge1\), and the seven-eighths import, taken as a hypothesis, would give it on \(\Re s>7/8\); the identification of that sum with \(\Re\xi'/\xi\) is the classical partial-fraction formula, used here from memory and not relied on.

The real-line form. The phase of \(p+ip'=p(1+ip'/p)\) on the real line has derivative \((p'/p)'/(1+(p'/p)^2)=-\mathcal L/(1+(p'/p)^2)\) between real roots, so monotonicity of the phase is \(\mathcal L\ge0\), the Laguerre inequality. This is only the local half of the criterion on the real line. The other half is global: the classical criterion, as I recall its statement and without a source read in this cycle, asks that the zeros of \(p\) and \(p'\) be real, simple and interlacing, which requires \(p\) of degree \(n\) to have \(n\) real zeros; a configuration with an off-axis pair has \(n-2\), so the count detects the pair at every \(\delta\), and the count is the statement that all points are real. By (b) an off-axis pair violates the local condition at its own abscissa only when \(2m/\delta^2\) exceeds the real points' sum there, and nowhere when the real points near it have gaps at most \(2\delta/\sqrt m\). For points with gaps comparable to \(1/D\) at density \(D\) per unit height, the local condition can therefore fail only for \(\delta\) below about \(1/D\): for the zeros near height \(T\), below about \(1/\log T\), under an assumption of regular gaps that is not known. The Gram form's extreme eigenvalues are proved to see a solitary pair when \(\delta\) is above about \(\log\log T/\log T\), in the flat-window complete-grid model under its local-count hypothesis (extreme eigenvalue detection); nothing is proved about their blindness below that. Between the two scales neither statement gives detection. Neither criterion has an available estimate behind it.

What is not examined. Higher-order conditions on the real line (the inequalities coming from higher derivatives of \(p'/p\)) are not treated; that at a real point they see only the nearest singularities of \(p'/p\) is an untested hypothesis, and nothing rests on it. The general Hermite–Biehler theorem, the class of structure functions and the Hilbert spaces attached to them are not read; the lead names them, and nothing here speaks to what they contain beyond the polynomial case.

Sub-ideas

  • Read a source on structure functions of de Branges type for \(\xi\) (the construction \(E=\xi+\xi'\) in a suitable normalization) in its text, and state exactly which condition on \(E\) is equivalent to the goal and whether any part of it is known unconditionally on a region larger than what part (a) gives from a zero-free half-plane.
  • Find why a reply that withdraws an earlier claim without the Stop hook's trigger words passes without a Prevention paragraph, and extend the hook's patterns (withdrawn, corrected, was wrong as worded) with a test, measuring false refusals on the session's past replies.

Follow-up. Continuing: the lead asked whether the Hermite–Biehler form gives a criterion of a different kind. For finite configurations its half-plane form and its real-line count are the zero-free statement, and its local real-line condition does not fail beyond half the local gap. That would close the lead as a restatement for polynomials, but the lead names sources I have not read, and a closure may not rest on that; the sub-idea above is the next attempt. Record search. For that attempt the registry was searched for Laguerre inequality logarithmic derivative positivity half-plane zeros, and for Hermite Biehler stable interlacing derivative: no record on these objects; the registry's interlacing statements concern other polynomial families (conj:cube-tie-window-interlacing, lem:cube-circle-polynomial).

Reviewer's suggestions, unverified

  • The known criteria of this type for \(\xi\) (a structure function \(\xi+\xi'\) in the variable \(z\); shifted functions \(\xi(1/2+h-iz)\), unconditional for \(h\ge1/2\)) are all equivalent to part (a), so the unconditional part is exactly the half-plane \(\Re s\ge1\). From the reviewer's memory, no source read. Record. Search for de Branges structure function Hermite-Biehler xi returned no record on these objects; this is what the sub-idea's reading is to confirm.
  • Monotone phase on a vertical line, monotone modulus along horizontal lines and part (a) are one condition, since \(\partial_t\arg f(\sigma+it)=\Re f'/f=\partial_\sigma\log|f|\). Record. Search for monotone modulus horizontal phase vertical line logarithmic derivative positivity returned no record on these objects.
  • A solitary zero to the right of a line makes \(\Re\xi'/\xi\) negative only in a disc of diameter about \(2m/\log T\) to its left, so positivity on one line is weaker than a zero-free half-plane, should hold outside a set of small measure by density estimates, and cannot be decided by averaged estimates; the disc statement would be the natural finite-configuration lemma. Record. The same two searches returned nothing on it; not added to the queue in this entry.
  • For real-line conditions the relevant distinction is local against global, not the order of the derivative: only the count sees a far pair. Record. No record; adopted in the text above as the description of the two halves.

Suggestion check. The second suggestion's identity is verified: \(\partial_t\log f(\sigma+it)=if'/f\), whose imaginary part is \(\Re f'/f\), and \(\partial_\sigma\log f=f'/f\), whose real part is \(\Re f'/f\). The reviewer's calibration, that on a lattice of spacing \(g\) with \(\gamma\) at a midpoint (i) gives failure exactly for \(\delta<\sqrt2g/\pi\), is verified for simple points: \(\sum_{k}(g(k+1/2))^{-2}=\pi^2/g^2\), and \(\pi^2/g^2<2/\delta^2\) is \(\delta<\sqrt2g/\pi\). The others are not verified and nothing rests on them.

Verification gate. Dependencies re-read: the Thresholds paragraph of extreme eigenvalue detection. One fresh-context medium reviewer checked parts (a) and (b) with their proofs and the degenerate cases and found them correct, and required corrections to the interpretation, all incorporated: the zeros of \(p+ip'\) include multiple roots of \(p\); the translation of (a) to real points needs closure under conjugation; a sign was stated the wrong way round in the summary; phase monotonicity is only the local half of the real-line criterion, the count of real zeros being the other half, which detects every pair and is the goal's statement; "sees only when" misdescribed the earlier entry, which proves detection above a scale and nothing below it; the two ranges leave a gap and are not complementary. 11 October 2026 — The structure-function condition at a positive shift holds exactly when no zero lies to the right of the shifted line: proved for finite configurations and derived for \(\xi\) from a source's proof; the Hermite–Biehler lead stays open on what the source reports about the zeros of the sum functions

Working proof of an elementary equivalence for finite configurations, with a reading of one arXiv source's statements and proof for \(\xi\); the version for \(\xi\) rests on that source's product formula. Structure functions. Produced by Claude Code (claude-opus-5-5, low effort).

Chosen. This entry continues the route review's second lead ahead of the queue's head, as the previous entry's next step named: the lead's outcome depended on reading a source on structure functions, and it bears on off-line exclusion by asking whether any part of the required condition is known unconditionally beyond a zero-free half-plane. At the goal's parameters it could give such a part; if the condition is the zero-free statement at every shift, it gives nothing and the lead closes.

Question. The missing implication is an estimate excluding one off-line zero. Which condition on a structure function attached to \(\xi\) is equivalent to the goal, and is any part of it known beyond what a zero-free half-plane gives? The test ends with the condition read from a source and compared with the finite-configuration statement, and with the lead closed or a condition of another kind found.

New mathematics. One elementary equivalence. For a finite multiset of points \(\rho\) with polynomial \(F\) and real \(h\): \(|F(h+s)|>|F(h+1-\bar s)|\) for all \(\Re s>1/2\) exactly when every point has \(\Re\rho\le1/2+h\) and at least one has \(\Re\rho<1/2+h\). It comes from the identity \(|h+s-\rho|^2-|h+1-\bar s-\rho|^2=(2\sigma-1)(2h+1-2\beta)\), which is also the step of the source's proof for \(\xi\); read with that identity, the source's argument gives the same equivalence for \(\xi\) at every \(h>0\). Elementary, and a small sharpening of the source's lemma, which states the cases \(h\ge1/2\) and the goal's hypothesis; no novelty is claimed.

Goal progress. Unchanged. The structure-function condition for the shifted function \(\xi(1/2+h-iz)\), as an inequality on the whole half-plane, is at each \(h>0\) the statement that no zero has real part above \(1/2+h\). What is known unconditionally of it: the whole condition for \(h\ge1/2\), and for every \(h>0\) the inequality at the points with \(\sigma\ge1\) (the modulus of \(\xi\) increases to the right there); both come from the zero-free half-plane \(\Re s\ge1\). With the seven-eighths import as a hypothesis, \(h\ge3/8\) and \(\sigma>7/8\). So the modulus inequality at a fixed shift, like the two forms of the previous entry, is the zero-free statement. Not examined: whether the consequences the source draws (zeros of the sum and difference functions on the critical line, interlacing) imply the condition back, and the source reports unconditional information of another kind about those zeros, a count with an error controlled by the number of zeros to the right of the shifted line; nor conditions on the inner product of the associated space, for which the source reports only that de Branges's sufficient conditions fail for \(\xi(1-iz)\). I drafted a closure of the lead and the review did not uphold it. Bearing. At the goal's parameters this gives nothing: the inequality is the goal's statement as \(h\to0\). The cycle was spent on it because the previous entry could not decide the lead without a source read.

General statement. prop:shifted-structure-criterion, for every nonempty finite multiset of complex points and every real \(h\).

What the source states

Read from the arXiv source of J. C. Lagarias, Zero spacing distributions for differenced L-functions, arXiv math/0601653 (its TeX file): Section 2 with the product formula, Lemma 2.1 with its proof, the statement of Lemma 2.2, Theorem 2.1 with its proof, and Section 6 through Lemma 6.1 and the remarks after it. Its statements, in its notation:

  • The modified product: \(\xi(s)=e^{A^*+B^*s}\prod_\rho(1-s/\rho)\), the product taken over \(|\rho|<T\) with \(T\to\infty\), and \(B^*=0\) (the source notes that \(\Re B^*\ge0\) is all its lemma needs).
  • Lemma 2.1: (1) if \(h\ge1/2\), then \(|\xi(h+s)|>|\xi(h+1-\bar s)|\) for \(\Re s>1/2\); (2) assuming the Riemann hypothesis, this holds for each \(h>0\). The proof is factor by factor: for a zero \(\rho=\beta+i\gamma\) and \(s=\sigma+it\) the factor inequality is equivalent to \(|\beta-h-\sigma|>|\beta-h-(1-\sigma)|\), shown in the two cases by a strict triangle inequality.
  • Lemma 2.2 (attributed there to de Branges): an entire \(E\) with \(|E(s)|>|E(1-\bar s)|\) for \(\Re s>1/2\) has \(A=\tfrac12(E+E^\sharp)\) and \(B=-\tfrac1{2i}(E-E^\sharp)\), \(E^\sharp(s)=\overline{E(1-\bar s)}\), with all zeros on the critical line, interlacing.
  • Theorem 2.1: for \(|h|\ge1/2\), and under the Riemann hypothesis for \(0<|h|<1/2\), the functions \(A_{h,\theta}\), \(B_{h,\theta}\) built from \(e^{i\theta}\xi(s+h)\) have all zeros on the critical line, simple and interlacing.
  • Section 6: a de Branges structure function is an entire \(E\) with \(|E(z)|>|\overline{E(\bar z)}|\) for \(\Im z>0\); \(E_h(z)=\xi(1/2+h-iz)\) is one for \(h\ge1/2\) and, under the Riemann hypothesis, for all \(h\ne0\). The case \(h=1/2\) is the function de Branges considered. The text says that de Branges proved theorems giving sufficient conditions on the inner product of such a space for the structure function to have its zeros on a line, that the Riemann hypothesis would follow if one applied to the space of \(\xi(1-iz)\), and that Conrey and Li showed their hypotheses are not satisfied for it; and that another paper of the same author gives a space that exists if and only if the Riemann hypothesis holds. It adds that spaces satisfying de Branges's conditions do exist.
  • Introduction and Section 3: a paper of H. Ki is reported to prove, for the sum function built from \(\pi^{-s/2}\Gamma(s/2)\zeta(s)\) in place of \(\xi\), that all zeros lie on the critical line for \(h\ge1/2\), and for every \(h>0\) a count \(\tfrac T{2\pi}\log\tfrac T{2\pi}-\tfrac T{2\pi}+O(\tilde R_h(T)\log T)\) of its zeros up to height \(T\), where \(\tilde R_h(T)\) counts zeros of \(\zeta\) with real part above \(1/2+h\); the source says the methods apply to \(\xi\) as well.

The section and lemma numbers are mine, counted from the TeX, whose labels differ. One statement of the source is wrong as written: its Section 6 lemma says that under the Riemann hypothesis \(E_h\) is a structure function for all \(h\ne0\); for \(h<0\) the identity below reverses every factor's inequality, and the lemma's proof, a restatement of Lemma 2.1, covers \(h>0\) only. Not read: de Branges's papers, the paper of Conrey and Li, the paper of Ki, the other paper of the same author, and the proof of Lemma 2.2.

Statement and proof

Proposition (shifted structure criterion). prop:shifted-structure-criterion. Let \(R\) be a nonempty finite set of complex numbers \(\rho=\beta+i\gamma\) with multiplicities \(m_\rho\ge1\), \(F(s)=\prod_\rho(s-\rho)^{m_\rho}\), and \(h\) real. Then \[|F(h+s)|>|F(h+1-\bar s)|\quad\text{for all }s\text{ with }\Re s>\tfrac12\] if and only if every \(\rho\in R\) has \(\beta\le\tfrac12+h\) and at least one has \(\beta<\tfrac12+h\).

Proof. For \(s=\sigma+it\), the numbers \(h+s-\rho\) and \(h+1-\bar s-\rho\) have the same imaginary part \(t-\gamma\) and real parts \(h+\sigma-\beta\) and \(h+1-\sigma-\beta\), so \[|h+s-\rho|^2-|h+1-\bar s-\rho|^2=(h+\sigma-\beta)^2-(h+1-\sigma-\beta)^2=(2\sigma-1)(2h+1-2\beta).\] For \(\sigma>1/2\) this is positive, zero or negative according as \(\beta\) is below, at or above \(1/2+h\). If all \(\beta\le1/2+h\), every factor of \(|F(h+s)|\) is at least the corresponding factor of \(|F(h+1-\bar s)|\), and \(|F(h+s)|>0\) because \(\Re(h+s)>1/2+h\ge\beta\) for all \(\rho\); with one \(\beta<1/2+h\), that factor is strictly larger and the product inequality is strict (if \(F(h+1-\bar s)=0\) it holds because the left side is positive). Conversely, if some \(\beta_0>1/2+h\), then \(s=\rho_0-h\) has \(\Re s>1/2\) and \(F(h+s)=0\), so the strict inequality fails there; and if all \(\beta=1/2+h\), both sides are equal. \(\square\)

Degenerate cases. The empty set is excluded: \(F\equiv1\) gives equality, in line with the condition "at least one" failing. All points on the line \(\beta=1/2+h\): equality everywhere, not a structure function; this is why the unshifted function of a configuration on the critical line is not one, as for \(h=0\) in the source. Negative \(h\) is allowed in the statement and simply moves the line to the left. A set symmetric under \(\rho\mapsto1-\bar\rho\) with \(h>0\) and all \(\beta\le1/2+h\) always has a point with \(\beta<1/2+h\), since \(\beta\) and \(1-\beta\) cannot both equal \(1/2+h\). No symmetry is needed for the proposition.

The version for \(\xi\), and what it closes

For \(\xi\), on the source's product formula. The source's factor inequality \(|\beta-h-\sigma|>|\beta-h-(1-\sigma)|\) is, by the identity above, equivalent to \(\beta<1/2+h\) when \(\sigma>1/2\). So its proof gives more than its two cases: if no zero of \(\xi\) has \(\beta>1/2+h\), with \(h>0\), then each added factor in the partial products over \(|\rho|<T\) multiplies the ratio \(|P_T(h+s)|/|P_T(h+1-\bar s)|\) by a number at least \(1\), the ratio is at least that of a single factor with \(\beta<1/2+h\) (one exists by the symmetry \(\rho\mapsto1-\bar\rho\)), and the limit gives \(|\xi(h+s)|>|\xi(h+1-\bar s)|\) when the right side is not zero; when it is zero the inequality holds because \(\xi(h+s)\ne0\). The exponential factor has modulus ratio \(1\) as \(B^*=0\); and \(\xi\) has a zero, since by the same formula it would otherwise be constant. Conversely the inequality implies \(\xi(h+s)\ne0\) for \(\Re s>1/2\). Hence, relying on the source's product formula as read: for \(h>0\), \(E_h(z)=\xi(1/2+h-iz)\) is a structure function if and only if \(\xi\) has no zero with real part above \(1/2+h\). For \(h\le0\) it never is one: at \(h=0\) the two sides are equal by the functional equation, and for \(h<0\) a zero with \(\beta\ge1/2>1/2+h\) exists. This equivalence is my extension of the source's proof; the source states the cases \(h\ge1/2\) and the goal's hypothesis.

The inequality at one point is weaker. By \(\rho\mapsto1-\bar\rho\) the right side is \(|\xi(s-h)|\), and factor by factor \(|s+h-\rho|^2-|s-h-\rho|^2=4h(\sigma-\beta)\). So the inequality at a point \(s\) holds whenever \(\sigma>\beta\) for all zeros, in particular unconditionally for every \(h>0\) at \(\sigma\ge1\), and it may hold at points to the left of a zero when the other factors compensate. The statement that a zero to the right of the shifted line cannot be cancelled is true at the point \(s=\rho_0-h\) only, which is enough for the equivalence and for nothing pointwise elsewhere.

A conditional consequence, not used. With the seven-eighths import as a hypothesis (no zero with \(\Re s>7/8\), the boundary not excluded), \(E_h\) is a structure function for every \(h\ge3/8\), and the source's Lemma 2.2 then puts the zeros of \(A_{h,\theta}\) and \(B_{h,\theta}\) on the critical line, interlacing, for \(h\ge3/8\), simple for \(h>3/8\) by the source's argument. This depends on an unaudited import and on a lemma whose proof is not read; it is recorded as an illustration that such consequences follow from a zero-free half-plane, and it is not registered.

What this says about the lead. The lead proposed the Hermite–Biehler or structure-function form as a statement of a different kind. The forms examined so far are the zero-free statement: the half-plane sign of the logarithmic derivative and the count of real zeros (sign criteria), and now the modulus inequality on the whole half-plane at each positive shift. The reviewer gives the reason these must be so, which I record as a remark and not a theorem: an inequality that is checked factor by factor over the product has each factor's sign fixed by the position of one zero against a line, so the whole condition is a statement about zero positions and its unconditional part is what \(\beta<1\) gives. The local phase condition is weaker and, by the previous entry, for one pair among real points with gaps at most \(2\delta/\sqrt m\) around it, does not fail at all. What the lead still has: the consequence side. The source reports unconditional information about the zeros of the sum function at shifts \(0<h<1/2\), a count with an error \(O(\tilde R_h(T)\log T)\), and I have not asked whether the zeros of the sum and difference functions determine the condition, as they do for polynomials by the Hermite–Biehler theorem.

Sub-ideas

  • Quantify the consequence side for finite configurations: for a polynomial \(F\) with exactly \(k\) points to the right of the shifted line, bound the number of zeros of \(A_h=\tfrac12(F(s+h)+F^\sharp(s+h))\) off the critical line in terms of \(k\) (the generalized Hermite–Biehler count), and decide the converse: whether all zeros of \(A_{h,\theta}\) on the line for every \(\theta\) forces \(k=0\). Then read the statement of Ki's theorem (J. Number Theory 107, 2004) and compare: an unconditional bound for off-line zeros of the sum function by \(\tilde R_h(T)\) is a density statement, and the question is whether the converse gives exclusion or only density.
  • Retrieve and read the statement of the paper of Conrey and Li on de Branges's positivity conditions (reported by the reviewer, from memory, as arXiv math/9812166), so that the report that those conditions fail for \(\xi(1-iz)\) becomes a read statement, with the exact condition that fails.

Follow-up. Continuing: the lead asked whether the Hermite–Biehler form gives a criterion of another kind. The modulus inequality at a fixed shift does not; the consequence side and the inner-product conditions are not examined, and the first has unconditional partial information reported in the read source. Next attempt on this lead: the first sub-idea. Record search. For it the registry was searched for structure function inequality modulus reflected zeros half-plane interlace, and for Jensen polynomial hyperbolicity Turan inequalities de Bruijn Newman: both return only the imported half-plane statements and unrelated claims; no record of a count of off-line zeros of a sum function. The lead's earlier sub-ideas:

  • It asked to read a source on structure functions of de Branges type for \(\xi\) and to state which condition is equivalent to the goal and whether any part is known unconditionally beyond a zero-free half-plane. Read as listed above, for the shifted functions: the condition at shift \(h>0\) is the absence of zeros to the right of \(1/2+h\). The construction \(\xi+\xi'\) that the sub-idea names from my memory is not in the lines read and its paper was not retrieved; the suggestion check below reduces its defining inequality to the half-plane sign, which answers the sub-idea's question for that function by a computation and not by a reading. Reviewer's suggestions, unverified

  • The function \(E=\xi+\xi'\) has \(|E(s)|>|E(1-\bar s)|\) on \(\Re s>1/2\) exactly when \(\Re\xi'/\xi>0\) there, so that structure-function condition is the half-plane sign form. Record. prop:log-derivative-sign-criteria is its finite model; verified below.
  • The modulus inequality is the integral of the sign form: \(\log|\xi(s+h)|-\log|\xi(s-h)|=\int_{\sigma-h}^{\sigma+h}\Re\tfrac{\xi'}{\xi}(u+it)\,du\), which explains why it fails only near a zero. Record. The previous entry's suggestion list has the same identity in differential form; no other record.
  • H. Suggestion check. The first is verified: from \(\xi(1-s)=\xi(s)\), \(\xi'(1-s)=-\xi'(s)\), and \(\xi\) is real on the real axis, so \(E(1-\bar s)=\overline{\xi(s)-\xi'(s)}\) and \(|\xi+\xi'|^2-|\xi-\xi'|^2=4\Re(\xi'\bar\xi)=4|\xi|^2\Re(\xi'/\xi)\). Hence the inequality on \(\Re s>1/2\) holds exactly when \(\xi\ne0\) and \(\Re\xi'/\xi>0\) there (at a zero both sides equal \(|\xi'|\)). The second follows from \(\partial_\sigma\log|\xi|=\Re\xi'/\xi\) away from zeros. The others are not verified and nothing rests on them.

Verification gate. Dependencies re-read: the sign criteria entry; the source in the passages listed. One fresh-context medium reviewer checked the proposition and the version for \(\xi\) and found both correct for \(h>0\), and did not uphold the draft's closure of the lead. Its corrections, all incorporated: the source's lemma for all \(h\ne0\) is false for negative \(h\), by the draft's own identity, and the draft had reported it without comment; the sentence that no part of the condition is weaker than the absence of the zero was false, the inequality at one point being weaker and known unconditionally for \(\sigma\ge1\); "do not feed back" was unproved and is now an open question; the unconditional range was stated incompletely; the conditional consequence is restricted to positive shifts; the title said the equivalence was read where it is derived from the source's proof. Process assessment. For the second time in three entries I drafted a closure that the reviewer did not uphold: the reading supported one form of the lead and I wrote it as the whole lead, while the read source itself contained an unconditional statement of another kind that I passed over. Prevention. The class is closing an item on the forms examined while its statement names others. The finisher asks a closure for Stakes, Sources read and the reviewer's record, and the reviewer read the closure, which is what stopped it both times; before review nothing asks the author to list the item's named objects against what was examined. I add no mechanical check in this turn and queue none: the reviewer's reading is the existing check and it worked, and a check of an item's wording against an entry's would be a keyword match I cannot show to be reliable without measuring it.

11 October 2026 — Li's coefficients for finite configurations: an off-line pair at height \(\gamma\) and distance \(\delta\) lowers the \(n\)-th coefficient by at most about one unit while \(n\le\gamma^2/\delta\), and makes infinitely many coefficients negative beyond

Working proof of an elementary statement about finite configurations; the lead's sources are not read and it stays open. Li threshold. Produced by Claude Code (claude-opus-5-5, low effort).

Chosen. This entry takes the route review's third lead, Li's criterion, ahead of the queue's head: the first two leads have each had two consecutive entries (the queue tool's status at the starts of those cycles), this lead has had none (its status at this cycle's start), and it is the remaining queued lead that is a positivity statement about a sequence, bearing on off-line exclusion. At the goal's parameters it could give a positivity that an off-line zero at height \(T\) violates at an index \(n\) where something is known about the coefficients; if the index needed grows like \(T^2\) with nothing known there, it gives nothing.

Question. The missing implication is an estimate excluding one off-line zero. For a finite configuration closed under the two symmetries, with coefficients \(\lambda_n=\sum_\rho m_\rho(1-(1-1/\rho)^n)\): at which \(n\) can an off-line pair make \(\lambda_n\) negative, by how much does it lower \(\lambda_n\) before that, and can pairs cancel? The test ends with a proved threshold.

New mathematics. One elementary proposition. With \(\tau_\rho=\delta_\rho/(\min(\beta_\rho,1-\beta_\rho)^2+\gamma_\rho^2)\) for an off-line point at distance \(\delta_\rho\) from the line: \(\lambda_n\ge S_{\mathrm{on}}(n)-4\sum_{\mathrm{off}}m_\rho\sinh^2(n\tau_\rho/2)\), where \(S_{\mathrm{on}}(n)\ge0\) is the on-line points' contribution; in particular for \(n\le1/\tau_{\max}\) the off-line pairs lower \(\lambda_n\) by less than \(1.09\) per pair; and if there is any off-line point, \(\lambda_n\to-\infty\) along infinitely many \(n\). The last part is the finite model of the criterion's hard direction, which I believe is known in general (Bombieri and Lagarias, unread); no novelty is claimed.

Goal progress. Unchanged. In the model, positivity of all coefficients is equivalent to all points on the line, with no cancellation among pairs in the limit, but a negative coefficient at index \(n\) needs \(n\tau_{\max}>\log(S_{\mathrm{on}}(n)/M_{\mathrm{off}})\), \(\tau_{\max}\) being the largest \(\delta/\gamma^2\) of the configuration. So, provided the on-line contribution \(S_{\mathrm{on}}(n)\) exceeds \(M_{\mathrm{off}}e^{n\tau_{\max}}\) on the range, which is assumed and not derived for \(\zeta\), positivity of \(\lambda_n\) for \(n\le N\) constrains only configurations with some pair of \(\gamma^2/\delta\lesssim N\). Without that proviso the statement is false: a quadruple with no on-line points has a negative coefficient already at \(n\) of order \(\gamma\). For a zero at height \(T\) the range is \(n\gtrsim T^2\). That nothing is known about the coefficients there short of the zeros' location is an untested hypothesis of mine; the reviewer recalls, unread, a theorem that a one-sided subexponential lower bound for the coefficients is already equivalent to the goal, which would make any such bound a restatement. Bearing. At the goal's parameters this gives nothing: it locates the index range in which the criterion would have to be verified and supplies no means of verifying it. The cycle was spent on it because the lead proposes positivity of a sequence, a statement of another kind than the forms examined, and its first test had not been made.

General statement. prop:li-coefficient-threshold, for every finite multiset of complex points other than \(0\) and \(1\) that is closed, with multiplicities, under \(\rho\mapsto\bar\rho\) and \(\rho\mapsto1-\bar\rho\), and every \(n\ge1\).

Statement and proof

Let \(R\) be such a multiset, \(\rho=\beta+i\gamma\), \(w_\rho=1-1/\rho=(\rho-1)/\rho\), and \(\lambda_n=\sum_{\rho\in R}m_\rho(1-w_\rho^n)\). A point is on-line if \(\beta=1/2\). For an off-line point put \(\delta_\rho=|\beta-1/2|\), \(r_\rho=\max(|w_\rho|,|w_\rho|^{-1})>1\) and \(\tau_\rho=\delta_\rho/(\min(\beta,1-\beta)^2+\gamma^2)\). Sums run over distinct points with their multiplicities. The map \(\rho\mapsto1-\bar\rho\) is an involution whose fixed points are the on-line points, so the off-line points fall into partner pairs \(\{\rho,1-\bar\rho\}\) of equal multiplicity; a sum over off-line pairs takes one point from each, and a quadruple \(\rho,\bar\rho,1-\rho,1-\bar\rho\) is two pairs.

Proposition (Li coefficient threshold). prop:li-coefficient-threshold.

  1. \(\lambda_n\) is real and \[\lambda_n=S_{\mathrm{on}}(n)+\sum_{\text{off-line pairs}}m_\rho\bigl(2-(r_\rho^{\,n}+r_\rho^{-n})\cos n\varphi_\rho\bigr),\qquad S_{\mathrm{on}}(n)=\sum_{\text{on-line}}m_\rho(1-\cos n\theta_\rho)\ \ge0,\] where \(w_\rho=e^{i\theta_\rho}\) on the line and \(w_\rho=|w_\rho|e^{i\varphi_\rho}\) off it.
  2. \(\log r_\rho\le\tau_\rho\), and \[\lambda_n\ \ge\ S_{\mathrm{on}}(n)-4\sum_{\text{off-line pairs}}m_\rho\sinh^2(n\tau_\rho/2).\] In particular, for \(n\le1/\max_\rho\tau_\rho\), \(\lambda_n\ge S_{\mathrm{on}}(n)-4\sinh^2(\tfrac12)M_{\mathrm{off}}\), with \(M_{\mathrm{off}}\) the number of off-line pairs with multiplicity and \(4\sinh^2(\tfrac12)<1.09\); and if \(\lambda_n<0\) then \(M_{\mathrm{off}}\ge1\) and \(n\max_\rho\tau_\rho>\log(S_{\mathrm{on}}(n)/M_{\mathrm{off}})\), the logarithm of \(0\) read as \(-\infty\).
  3. If \(R\) has an off-line point, then \(\lambda_n<0\) for infinitely many \(n\), and \(\lambda_n\to-\infty\) along a sequence. If it has none, \(\lambda_n=S_{\mathrm{on}}(n)\ge0\) for all \(n\).

Proof. (1) \(|\rho-1|^2-|\rho|^2=1-2\beta\), so \(|w_\rho|=1\) exactly on the line. For the partner \(\rho'=1-\bar\rho\), \(w_{\rho'}=-\bar\rho/(1-\bar\rho)=\bar\rho/(\bar\rho-1)=1/\overline{w_\rho}\), which has modulus \(|w_\rho|^{-1}\) and the same argument \(\varphi_\rho\). So an off-line pair contributes \(m_\rho(2-(r^n+r^{-n})e^{in\varphi_\rho})\), and an on-line point, its own partner, \(m_\rho(1-e^{in\theta_\rho})\). Closure under conjugation, which sends \(w\) to \(\bar w\), makes the sum real, so it equals the sum of the real parts. (2) Take the point of the pair with \(\beta>1/2\); then \(r^2=|\rho|^2/|\rho-1|^2=1+(2\beta-1)/((1-\beta)^2+\gamma^2)\) and \(\log r\le\tfrac12(2\beta-1)/((1-\beta)^2+\gamma^2)=\tau_\rho\), the minimum in \(\tau_\rho\) being \(1-\beta\). Since \(\cos\le1\), \(2-(r^n+r^{-n})\cos n\varphi\ge2-r^n-r^{-n}=-(r^{n/2}-r^{-n/2})^2=-4\sinh^2(\tfrac n2\log r)\ge-4\sinh^2(n\tau_\rho/2)\). For \(n\tau_\rho\le1\) each term is at least \(-4\sinh^2(1/2)\). If \(\lambda_n<0\), then \(S_{\mathrm{on}}(n)<4M_{\mathrm{off}}\sinh^2(n\tau_{\max}/2)<M_{\mathrm{off}}e^{n\tau_{\max}}\), using \(4\sinh^2(x/2)<e^{x}\). (3) Let \(r_*>1\) be the largest \(r_\rho\) and \(P\) the set of off-line pairs attaining it, with total multiplicity \(M_*\). By the pigeonhole principle applied to the points \((n\varphi_\rho/2\pi)_{\rho\in P}\) of a torus (Dirichlet's simultaneous approximation; if all the \(\varphi_\rho/2\pi\) are rational, take multiples of a common denominator) there are infinitely many \(n\) with \(n\varphi_\rho\) within \(\pi/3\) of a multiple of \(2\pi\) for every pair in \(P\); for these, \(\sum_Pm_\rho(r_*^n+r_*^{-n})\cos n\varphi_\rho\ge\tfrac12M_*r_*^n\). All other terms of (1) are bounded in absolute value by \(2|R|+2|R|r_2^{\,n}\), where \(|R|\) counts with multiplicity and \(r_2<r_*\) is the next largest modulus, or \(1\). Hence \(\lambda_n\le2|R|(1+r_2^{\,n})+2M_*-\tfrac12M_*r_*^n\to-\infty\) along these \(n\). The last sentence is (1). \(\square\)

Degenerate cases. The empty configuration has \(\lambda_n=0\). A configuration of one off-line pair and its conjugates, with no on-line points, has \(S_{\mathrm{on}}=0\) and (2) gives no positivity: indeed \(\lambda_n<0\) as soon as \(\cos n\varphi\) is close enough to \(1\), by an amount at most \(4m\sinh^2(n\tau/2)\) per pair. Real off-line points (\(\gamma=0\), \(0<\beta<1\)) are allowed: there \(w<0\), \(\varphi=\pi\), and the pair's term is negative for every even \(n\). Real points outside \([0,1]\) are allowed by the statement, with \(\varphi=0\), and their pair's term is negative for every \(n\ge1\). The points \(0\) and \(1\) are excluded: at \(0\), \(w\) is undefined, and at \(1\), \(w=0\) and the partner \(0\) has none. When \(S_{\mathrm{on}}(n)\le M_{\mathrm{off}}\) the last inequality of (2) is vacuous. Closure under conjugation is used only for realness: parts 2 and 3 hold for \(\Re\lambda_n\) without it.

What it says

The index at which a pair matters. For a pair at height \(\gamma\) and distance \(\delta\) from the line, \(\tau\le\delta/\gamma^2\). Below \(n=\gamma^2/\delta\) the pair lowers \(\lambda_n\) by less than \(1.09m\) below what it would be without the pair's term (compared with the same pair moved onto the line, whose two points contribute between \(0\) and \(4m\), the deficit can be up to \(5.09m\)). For \(\lambda_n\) to be negative the index must exceed \(\tau_{\max}^{-1}\log(S_{\mathrm{on}}(n)/M_{\mathrm{off}})\), with \(\tau_{\max}^{-1}\) at least the smallest \(\gamma^2/\delta\) among the pairs; this says something only when \(S_{\mathrm{on}}(n)>M_{\mathrm{off}}\). So positivity of the coefficients up to \(N\) says nothing about a configuration all of whose pairs have \(\gamma^2/\delta\) well above \(N\), whenever the on-line contribution at those indices exceeds the number of off-line pairs. How large \(S_{\mathrm{on}}(n)\) is for the zeros of \(\zeta\) I do not derive; \(\theta_\rho=\pi-2\arctan2\gamma\) is about \(1/\gamma\), on-line points of height below about \(n\) contribute order one each and those above about \(n^2/(2\gamma^2)\) each, and the reviewer recalls the size \((n/2)\log n\); none of this is used.

No cancellation in the limit, at an unreachable index. Part 3 shows that pairs cannot cancel each other for all \(n\): the largest modulus wins along a sequence of indices on which the leading pairs' phases align. Such indices recur with bounded gaps, but the first alignment can be far beyond \(1/\tau\) when several pairs share the largest modulus. Along them \(\lambda_n\le-(\tfrac12-o(1))M_*r_*^n\): a finite configuration with an off-line point violates every lower bound \(\lambda_n\ge-ce^{\epsilon n}\) with \(\epsilon<\log r_*\). The criterion thus has the continuation criterion's virtue only asymptotically, and at indices of order \(T^2/\delta\) for a zero at height \(T\).

Comparison with the other forms. In the Gram form an off-line pair's weight grows like \(e^{\delta\Lambda}\) in the bandwidth and is visible, against local counts, at \(\Lambda\) of order \(\log T\); here its weight grows like \(e^{n\delta/\gamma^2}\) in the index. The two are the same exponential in different parametrizations, \(n/\gamma^2\) playing the role of \(\Lambda\): an index \(n\) probes, at height \(\gamma\), the bandwidth \(n/\gamma^2\), and the bandwidth \(\log T\) corresponds to \(n\) of order \(T^2\log T\), the logarithm of part 2. I state this as an analogy read off the two formulas, not as a transfer of any estimate.

Sub-ideas

  • Read a source for the arithmetic expression of the coefficients for \(\zeta\) (the formula of Bombieri and Lagarias through the Weil explicit formula, or an arXiv treatment of Li coefficients) in its statements, and record which sum over primes and which archimedean term \(\lambda_n\) equals, what is known unconditionally about \(\lambda_n\) for large \(n\), and whether any one-sided bound at \(n\) of order \(T^2\) is weaker than a zero-free statement at height \(T\). Also decide whether \(\lambda_n\) is the Weil functional at a specific test function, in which case Li's positivity is an instance of the positivity of the first lead and the analogy with the bandwidth is an identity. Not done in this entry: the paper is not on arXiv to my knowledge and locating a readable source is a cycle's reading, which the lead's next entry is for.

Follow-up. Continuing: the lead asked whether Li's positivity gives a criterion of another kind. For finite configurations it is equivalent to all points being on the line, with the threshold index of part 2. Whether anything is known about the coefficients at the indices that matter is not examined, and the lead's sources are unread; that is the sub-idea and the next attempt on this lead. Record search. For it the registry was searched for Li criterion coefficients positivity off-line zero threshold growth, and for Keiper Bombieri Lagarias lambda_n explicit formula test function Weil positivity: no record on these objects. Within this notebook the nearest records are the Gram form's positivity statements (lem:pair-atom-spectrum, prop:extreme-eigenvalue-detection); whether Li's coefficients are an instance of them is the sub-idea's second question.

Reviewer's suggestions, unverified

  • Bombieri and Lagarias, Theorem 1 (from memory): for a multiset with a convergence condition, all real parts at most \(1/2\) is equivalent to the real-part sums being nonnegative, and to their being at least \(-c(\epsilon)e^{\epsilon n}\) for every \(\epsilon>0\); so a one-sided subexponential bound is already the goal. Record. No registry record; its finite analogue is the last sentence added to the paragraph on cancellation, from part 3.

Record. No registry record; added to the sub-idea.
  • Lagarias and Voros (from memory): the archimedean part is \((n/2)\log n+cn+O(1)\) unconditionally, the prime part \(O(\sqrt n\log n)\) under the goal, with exponentially growing oscillation otherwise; so \(S_{\mathrm{on}}(n)\) is about \((n/2)\log n\) and the threshold is \(n\) about \((\gamma^2/\delta)\log n\). Record. Searched Keiper Bombieri Lagarias lambda_n explicit formula test function Weil positivity, and Li criterion coefficients positivity off-line zero threshold growth: no match on these objects.

Searched Keiper Bombieri Lagarias lambda_n explicit formula test function Weil positivity, and Li criterion coefficients positivity off-line zero threshold growth: no match on these objects.

Record. Searched Keiper Bombieri Lagarias lambda_n explicit formula test function Weil positivity, and Li criterion coefficients positivity off-line zero threshold growth: no match on these objects.
